# 09 — Implementation Plan

The agent works top to bottom. A milestone is done only when every task is `[x]` and `/ship-check`
passes. Each task lists the spec sections and `R-xx` IDs it must satisfy. Keep tasks small; split a
task rather than leaving it half-done. Add new tasks here when discovered — never keep them in your head.

Legend: `[ ]` todo · `[x]` done · `[~]` in progress (only one at a time).

Standing rule from M8.3 on (every later task, M9–M12 included): a new or changed user-facing string
ships in **en, de, fr and es in the same commit** — `test/policy/l10n_parity_test.dart` fails CI on a
missing, extra or untranslated key, a dropped placeholder or a changed select case. Follow the copy
rules and glossary in `03-ux-spec.md` §5. After any ARB change run
`dart run test/support/pseudo/write_pseudo.dart` (from `app/`; a test says when it is stale), and add
every new screen to `test/core/l10n/locale_sweep_test.dart` (pseudo-locale at three widths + de/fr/es).

---

## Milestone 2 — Data layer (goal: encrypted local DB + repositories, no network yet)

- [x] M2.0 Raise the Dart SDK floor to `^3.8.0` (json_serializable 6.13 warns below it; unlocks null-aware elements). Expect a repo-wide `dart format` change to the tall style in its own commit.
- [x] M2.1 Drift schema mirroring 05 + sync columns + `change_log`, `sync_cursors`; SQLCipher key from secure storage; web WASM/OPFS variant behind the same interface. (06 §4, ADR-0003) **Decide first:** `drift`/`drift_dev` ≥ 2.32 require `sqlite3` 3.x, which builds SQLite via native-assets hooks and changes how SQLCipher is bundled (affects `sqlcipher_flutter_libs`, CLAUDE.md §2). Both are capped `<2.32` in `pubspec.yaml` until this is decided (M0.11). Also: `sqlcipher_flutter_libs` is end-of-life (`0.7.0+eol`), and `drift_flutter` pulls in `sqlite3_flutter_libs`, which clashes with `sqlcipher_flutter_libs` on Apple platforms (duplicate `Sqlite3FlutterLibsPlugin` → macOS build fails; found in M0.11).
- [x] M2.2 DAOs + repository implementations (`OccurrenceRepository`, `SeriesRepository`, `SubtaskRepository`, `SettingsRepository`) with reactive streams for day/inbox/week/month queries.
- [x] M2.3 Write path: every mutation goes through a `WriteUseCase` that updates DB, appends `change_log`, triggers `Materialiser` (if series), `NotificationPlanner` diff, widget snapshot write. Tests.
- [x] M2.4 `BackupService`: daily local JSON backup (keep 7), pre-migration backup; restore. Tests. (R-08, R-11) Lost key (M2.1): opening an existing file without its key, or with a key that doesn't decrypt it, throws a typed `DatabaseUnavailable`; no key is ever minted over an existing file. Local backups share the key, so recovery is via sync or a user export (M3.11 UI).
- [x] M2.5 Trash: soft delete, 30-day restore, purge; undo token API for UI (30 s). (R-11)
- [x] M2.6 Search index (FTS5) over title/notes/subtasks incl. completed. (R-11)
- [x] M2.7 Performance test: 10 000 occurrences, day query < 16 ms, search < 50 ms. (R-15)
- [x] M2.9 Web at-rest encryption per ADR-0003 (found in M2.1: the web DB is Drift WASM/OPFS, unencrypted). Decide between `sqlite3mc.wasm` keyed by a raw key that is stored wrapped by a non-extractable WebCrypto key in IndexedDB, and a custom encrypting VFS; amend ADR-0003; test that OPFS bytes don't contain a task title.
- [x] M2.8 `/ship-check`.

## Milestone 3 — UI, Concept A (goal: every v1 screen in `10-ui-concept-a.md` §5, local only, on all four targets)

Re-planned 2026-10-21 for ADR-0007. Source of truth: `docs/10-ui-concept-a.md` (contract) + `docs/design-references/png/` (renders), CLAUDE.md §11.
Each task names its reference render (`Ref`) or `derived — §5.3/5.4` when there is none.

**Done criteria.** **§7** means all six items of `10-ui-concept-a.md` §7: (1) golden vs the reference or spec at 1×/2×, light + dark;
(2) only `DotTheme` tokens and §3 components; (3) compact / medium / expanded, Mac ≡ web at the same width; (4) 08 §3 a11y plus
a `TaskDot` state label, 44/48 px targets, 4.5:1 contrast; (5) all copy from ARB; (6) a screenshot-sweep entry in 08 §7.
`+` adds the task's own criteria. Derived screens also get a row under "derived" in `docs/design-references/README.md`.

**Pre-Concept A work (kept, superseded visually).** M3.1 shared components, M3.1a demo day, M3.2 timeline canvas and app-start wiring,
M3.3 block widget — all `[x]`. Their state, layout maths, app-start wiring, `TimeInput`, `UndoSnackbar`, `SyncPill` and the
demo-day loader are reused; the hour-gutter canvas and block visuals are replaced by `Thread`/`TaskRow` in M3b.1.
M3.4 gestures (`4938eee` on `feat/m3-4-gestures`, never merged) targets the old canvas: it is not merged as-is; its snapping and
auto-scroll logic is reused in M3b.5.

**Discrepancies between renders and the doc (the doc wins; the PRD wins over both on product questions):**
- LiveRow in `Main`/`A-android-today` shows Done · +15 min only; §3 adds "Shift the rest" → built per §3.
- `A-ios-task-sheet` shows Done · +15 min · Move · Edit; §3 says Done / +15 / Shift → all five: Done · +15 · Shift · Move · Edit (Move = R-05, Edit = E1).
- `A-android-inbox` has filter chips (All / Under 30 min / Fits today) and a mic in the quick-add field; `A-ios-inbox` has neither.
  §4 allows no such difference → chips go on every platform (they serve R-17 "fits today"); the mic is dropped (no speech dependency in §2).
- `A-android-settings` rows have subtitles and the DAY SHAPE slider shows 7:00/22:00; the iOS one shows 00/24 and no subtitles →
  one layout on both: subtitles shown, times under the handles.
- The Search header ring shows a magnifier on iOS and the result count "4" on Android → the count on both (a dot ring holding loose dots, §1).
- The Search results use people/document icons that the original 24-icon set lacked → set expanded to 26 (owner, 2026-10-21; §3 updated).
- The `A-mac-editor` Save button shows ⌘S; §6 says ⏎ saves → ⏎ saves when focus is not in Notes, and ⌘S always saves.
- §5.3 Repeat editor: "this one or all future"; PRD F4 requires this / this and following / all → three scopes (PRD wins).
- Week start: `Main` starts its strip on Sunday while `A-ios-settings` says "Week starts Monday" → the strip follows Settings › Week starts (M3b.1).
- The Today overflow (⋯) shows on Android only in the renders → one ⋯ on every platform, beside the day dot (§4 lists no such idiom; it carries the debug menu now and Running late in M3f.5).
- `A-ios-inbox` shows "No duration" and mixes "60 min" with "2 h": `duration_min` is 5–1440 (schema) → an item without a
  typed length gets Settings › Default duration, and lengths use the §7.5 short form everywhere ("1 h", "1 h 30").
- Palette: §2.1 has 9 task colours; 03 §1 / `palette.json` have 12 → 9 (M3a.1). The stored `color_key` stays text (no schema
  change); legacy keys map on read: tangerine→amber, lime→mint, magenta→rose.

### M3c — Add flow

- [x] M3c.1 Add step 1 What A1: title with "Type a time too: 'Go shopping 5pm 1h'" (live `QuickAddParser` fill, R-06/R-20), `ColourIconBlock` (9 colours + 3 quick icons + "…"), Suggestions "from your habits" (a domain `SuggestionService`: same title → last used time, duration, colour, icon), Continue, "Add to Inbox instead"; ≤ 2 taps + typing from any tab. Ref `A-ios-add-what`, `A-android-add-what`. Done: §7 + a tap-count test and `SuggestionService` unit tests. Notes: two step dots (What, When) — the renders show three but §3 names two steps, so a third dot would point nowhere. One title-field style everywhere (label + underline, as the E1 editor; the renders' iOS underline vs Android outlined box is not a §4 idiom). Parsed pieces show as removable chips under the field (PRD F2) in place of the helper line. Suggestions: habits from the last 60 days (calendar imports excluded); while typing the list filters, and once the title names a habit the top four show with it ticked (`SuggestionService.shownFor`). Precedence of every value: a choice made in Add > typed > picked habit > settings default (`AddDraft`). Enter adds at once with what is known (timed → its day, else the Inbox), so FAB + typing + Enter is 1 tap; "Add to Inbox instead" is 2. The colour row scrolls instead of shrinking the 44 pt targets (as M3b.4b). The sheet closes before the write (F2 "optimistic") and confirms with Undo; a new series has no Undo yet (M3f.1).
- [x] M3c.2 Add step 2 When A2: `ThreadPicker` (scrolling slice through a fixed window, neighbours shown, the new segment in its colour, "Free until 19:30 · no overlap" / overlap warning), duration chips, "Lands on … · the slot is free · Edit", Add / Add another; also the Inbox schedule sheet's "Pick a time…" (deferred from M3b.8). Ref `A-ios-add-when`, `A-android-add-when`. Done: §7 + overlap-warning tests; the picker is keyboard-operable. Notes: the picker scrolls the day under a fixed window (0.7 pt/min, ~42 pt/h; the window is the task's length, at least one 22 px dot) and snaps to Settings › Snap; arrows ±snap, Page Up/Down ±1 h, a screen-reader slider with increase/decrease; the window's pill is excluded from semantics (the slider reads the range). Overlap is a warning, not a block ("Overlaps Gym · you can still add it"): overlapping is legal (R-04 lets the user shift later); domain `fitAt` counts travel buffers, ignores skipped and all-day items. With no typed time, Continue starts in the day's first free slot for the length (else now / Wake Up). Add another saves, keeps the sheet on step 1 and starts the next task where this one ends. Duration chips 15 30 45 1h 1.5h 2h share one row (they wrap above 130 % text); M3c.3 makes them the editable presets. "Lands on … Edit" and the TIME/DURATION ⋯ arrive with M3c.3/M3c.4. Inbox: the schedule sheet gains "Pick a time…" (today / tomorrow on the same picker, then Place). `dayRoom` moved to `data/scheduling` so Add and Inbox share it.

Notes: presets live in `settings.durationPresets` (1–12 distinct lengths of 5 min–24 h, default 15 30 45 1h 1.5h 2h; client-validated — the server's `validate_settings()` ignores unknown keys, so no migration); add / remove / Reset save at once through the write path (`updateSettings`), the last preset can't be removed. (Add N · Keep as one) as soon as a list is pasted — 03 §3.3, the more specific doc; Enter confirms. ### M3d — Search and Settings

Ref `A-ios-search`, `A-android-search`. Done: §7 + search < 50 ms on 10 000 rows through the provider. The Android render's ⋮ in the header is dropped (no overflow menu on Search; §4 lists none). Highlight = word-prefix matches like the FTS index (accents folded, domain `matchSpans`). RECENT = last 5 committed searches (submitted, or a result opened), on this device only in `local_meta` — search terms can be personal, so they are never synced or exported. Colour and Icon chips open a one-pick sheet; tapping the chip again clears it. "+ Schedule" runs the Inbox's schedule flow through a core `ScheduleTaskIntent` (no feature imports another); a result opens `/task/:id`. Empty and no-results states (§5.3) are built here, with "Show all" when a chip hid everything. `minuteTick` moved to `core/clock` (Search shows "38 min left"); the Inbox and Search headers share `CountRing`. Perf: provider-level search p95 11 ms on 10 000 rows (`db_performance_test`).

Notes: header reads "Kody · on this device" until accounts exist ("synced just now" arrives with sync, M6). DAY SHAPE edits the everyday window — the one most weekdays share; moving it moves every weekday that shares it, custom days keep theirs (domain `day_boundaries.dart`); "Different days" lists each weekday with its own slider and "Same as every day". The slider keeps a drag local and writes once on release; each handle is a keyboard/screen-reader slider (15-min steps, Page ±1 h); Bedtime stops at 23:45 (windows are `HH:mm` ≤ 23:59). Rows are 48 pt on iPhone, 56 dp elsewhere (§3); switches follow `DotIdiom` (Cupertino on iPhone, Material on Android, Mac and web) instead of `Switch.adaptive`, which would give the Mac and web-on-Mac the iPhone switch. New settings keys `showFreeTime` (drives the gap labels now), `runningLateNudge`, `rollUnfinished` (M3f.5) and `bedtimeReminder` (M3d.3/M5); no migration (unknown keys pass the server check). Theme, accent and language now apply app-wide; Language lists the bundled locales (English until M8.3). DATA has Trash (list + Restore; the full page with selection is M3f.7); Calendars and Export join with their pages (M3f.7). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: unit tests for each consumer with an injected Clock; DST day test. Notes: the day dot, thread caps, day page and the Add/Inbox room already read `DayShape.on(zone, date, settings.boundaryFor(weekday))` since M3a/M3c — this task adds what was missing. Free time is now domain `capacityOf`: the union of task footprints (travel buffers included) clipped to Wake Up–Bedtime, so overlaps count once and tasks outside the waking day are ignored (it used to subtract planned minutes, which double-counted overlaps); skipped and all-day tasks are excluded. Bedtime reminder: a planner *purpose* (`NotificationPurpose.bedtime`), not a new `AlertKind`, so no schema or validation change — one per local date at that weekday's Bedtime inside the 7-day horizon, stable id per date (`bedtime:y-m-d`, index 6), carrying the next day's first task id (tap opens it; none → Today), silent in quiet hours, never time-sensitive; on by default (Settings › Reminders). Tests: capacity (overlaps, buffers, outside, 23/25-hour Berlin DST days), Bedtime (DST change 25 Oct, per-weekday, off, tomorrow's first task, quiet hours, stable ids → empty diff, past Bedtime), and the day room provider per weekday; all on an injected `FakeClock`.

### M3f — Secondary screens and system states

- [x] M3f.1 Repeat editor: never / daily / weekdays / weekly on … / monthly / custom (RRULE builder, every N, nth weekday), end never / after N / on date, readable summary, edit/delete scope this / this and following / all, series undo (R-01). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7 + scope tests against `RecurrenceExpander`. Notes: domain `RepeatRule` (rule ↔ RRULE body, round-trip tested; anything it can't express — BYSETPOS, BYMONTH, several month days — stays "Custom" and is never rewritten) and `SeriesEditor` (scopes). Scope semantics: *this one* = the row (autosave already wrote it); *this and following* starts at the instance in hand; *all* starts at the first planned instance from today, so history never changes. Field changes (title, notes, colour, icon, length, pin) update the series in place and later planned rows. A new time or rule splits the series: the old one ends one second before the pivot (or is archived if nothing came before), a new one (new id) starts there; each later row the new rule still lands on moves over with its id and subtasks (no re-creation, so ticked subtasks survive); planned rows it no longer covers are soft-deleted; done/skipped rows stay as history. Using a new id avoids the materialiser treating soft-deleted rows as exceptions for the new rule. A counted series keeps its remaining count. Deletes: following ends the series and soft-deletes later planned rows; all deletes the series and planned rows from now. Every scoped write is one batch → one Undo. UI: the Repeat editor (`shared/pickers/repeat/`, Never / Every day / Every weekday / Every week on chips / Every month by date or nth weekday / Every year / Custom every N days-weeks-months; Ends never / after N / on a date; live summary) and "Change which?" / "Delete which?" sheets. The editor asks on Save only when a shared field changed against the instance as opened, and on Delete for repeating tasks (the choice is the confirmation); a repeat rule change asks only following / all; "Never" ends the series after this instance; a one-off choosing a rule becomes the first instance of a new series. `taskView` re-reads the series with each row change. Phone note: the Undo snackbar sits behind the editor sheet's barrier until the sheet closes (Undo stays available after closing).
- [x] M3f.2 Alerts editor: at start, N min before, at end, overdue, Bedtime reminder; up to 5; defaults from settings (R-03, R-10). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7 + `notification_planner_test` still green. Notes: `shared/pickers/alerts/` — the task's alerts with ×, choices 60/30/15/10/5 min before, at start, at end, 10 min after end (fixed set: free-form minutes stay out until someone asks), five at most, kept in time order; the Bedtime reminder switch is the global Settings one (it reminds every evening, not per task) and writes at once. A one-off keeps its alerts on its row; a repeating task asks this one / following / all — for following/all the series takes the alerts and later rows inherit (`alerts = null`), "this one" keeps them on the row (`SeriesChange.alerts`). Found while wiring it: Settings › Reminders' default alert never reached new tasks — `rowsFromDraft` / `AddDraft.toRows` now copy `settings.defaultAlerts` onto new one-offs and series (Add flow and Quick Add/Inbox field). `notification_planner_test` green.
- [x] M3f.3 Subtask editor (inline add, reorder, rename; template vs occurrence, R-09) + full colour & icon picker sheet (26-icon grid). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7. Notes: in the editor each subtask has its tick (this day only), tap-the-title rename (a small dialog — an inline field inside the reorderable list lost focus to the editor's time field a frame after opening), × remove (soft, with Undo), a drag handle and Move up / Move down for screen readers and keyboards (fractional `sortOrder`, one row written), and "now" on the next one while the task runs. The task sheet keeps tap-to-tick (`TaskSheet`). Template vs occurrence: edits stay on the day; a repeating task offers "Use this list every time" — the day's list becomes the series template and every later planned day with nothing ticked gets it fresh (soft-deleted + rebuilt), days with a tick keep theirs (completion is never overwritten, R-09); one batch, one Undo (domain `subtask_template.dart`). The full picker ("…") is one sheet — popover on wide windows, not the raw bottom sheet it was — with every colour and the 26 icons tinted in it; an icon picks both, Done keeps a colour alone.
- [x] M3f.4 Change-day picker (`MonthGrid` in a sheet), time-zone picker. Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7. Notes: one Change-day picker everywhere — the editor's WHEN, Add's Change day, Copy a day… and Move selection now all open the shared `showMonthDayPicker` (the timeline's own `day_picker_sheet.dart` is gone), with the user's first day of the week. Each day shows the colours of what is on it (up to 3, skipped tasks left out), past days included, so a free day — or the day to copy from — is easy to spot; the month's data moved from `features/timeline` to `data/schedule` so Add (another feature) can use it. The editor gets a "Time zone · Berlin" row under START: the shared zone picker, keeping the wall clock and moving the instant (as Add's Time zone, M3c.4).
- [x] M3f.5 "Running late" sheet (shift the rest / skip / shorten, +5/+15/+30, R-04) + Day review at Bedtime ("3 of 7 done · move the rest to tomorrow?"). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7 + pinned/partial tests. Notes: the sheet opens from Today's ⋯ (now shown on every platform on today, as planned in M3a; other days keep it for debug builds only) and from the spread's Running late button, which used to be a +5/+15/+30 menu. Pick how late, then: Shift the rest (what is left from now, stopping at the first pinned task — the preview names it before anything moves, R-17); Skip the task at hand (the running one, else the next; that day only); or Shorten it (starts N min later, same end, so nothing else moves; off when under 5 min would remain). Calendar events are never the task at hand. Each choice is one batch with Undo. Day review: from Bedtime (that weekday's), a card above today's thread — "2 of 13 done · Send the rest to the Inbox?" — or "Move the rest to tomorrow?" with Settings › Roll unfinished on; it moves unfinished one-offs (pinned ones too, at their own wall time) and leaves repeating tasks and calendar events, saying how many stay. Also any time from ⋯ › Review the day. The running-late nudge (Settings, on by default) is the same card before Bedtime: a task ended 5–60 min ago without a tick while more is planned later → "Running late? · Catch up" opens the sheet. "Not now" puts a card away for the day, for this session only (a suggestion isn't worth syncing).
- [x] M3f.6 Inbox swipe confirmations + Undo snackbar, Completed group expanded, Search empty / no results, Export day + Duplicate day entry points. Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7. Notes: swipe confirmations (the labels behind a swiping row, Undo for Trash, the Schedule sheet for the other way) and Search's empty / no-results states were already built in M3b.8 and M3d.1, and their tests still pass. New here: the open Completed group shows each done item's day ("Done Wed, Oct 21"), a ↺ that puts it back in the Inbox not done, and "Clear completed" (all to Trash); each has Undo, and the group stays open while rows change. The day's ⋯ now shows on every day: Duplicate day… picks the day to copy this day's one-off tasks onto (the same copy as an empty day's "Copy a day…", the other way round). Export day is "Copy day as text" for now: titles and times as plain text on the clipboard, on the user's say-so, never notes. The file export (CSV/ICS/JSON) is M8.1.
- [x] M3f.7 Settings sub-pages: Calendars (import list, static until M7), Export (static until M8.1), Help, Privacy, About, Trash (30-day restore, M2.5); Account page (sign in/out, delete with confirm — UI only, wired in M6.1/M6.5). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7. Notes: sub-pages are real routes, `/settings/<page>` (account, calendars, export, help, privacy, about), inside the Settings tab so the bar/rail stays. The deep-link allow-list (06 §7) accepts exactly those six names, and its tests cover the rejections. Settings › DATA = Calendars, Export, Trash (the Trash sheet with 30-day restore is unchanged from M3d.2). ABOUT = Help, Privacy, About Dottoday (version, What's new, open-source licences), Send feedback. The name/avatar header opens Account. Account: the name (edit, or clear), then signed out: Apple / Google / email buttons, off with "Sign-in arrives with sync" until M6.1. The `AccountService` interface (domain/auth) is what M4.5 and M6.1 build on; today's `LocalOnlyAccountService` is never signed in. Signed in (tested with a fake): Sign out, and Delete account behind a dialog that stays off until DELETE is typed (06 §6). Calendars: the empty connected list, Connect (off until M7) and one setting that works today, "Move calendar events too" (`includeCalendarInShift`). Export: the three formats to come, free; for now a day's ⋯ › Copy day as text. Help: five answers on the device (works offline) and Send feedback. Privacy: on device / no tracking / sync / searches, and Clear recent searches. New glyphs: person, share, help, info.
- [x] M3f.8 Sync status sheet (last synced, conflicts local vs remote, "keep mine / keep theirs" — UI on fakes, wired in M6.3), offline banner, notification-permission-denied states. Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7. Notes: the UI is built on two interfaces that M5/M6 implement. `SyncService` (domain/sync) gives state, last sync, pending changes, conflicts, Sync now and resolve; `NotificationPermission` (domain/notifications) gives status, request and open settings. Today's stand-ins are `LocalOnlySyncService`, always local-only, and `UnwiredNotificationPermission`, always granted, so nothing new shows in the app yet; the tests and goldens drive the fakes. The Sync sheet opens from Settings › DATA › Sync, whose value is the state or "N changes need you", through a core `ShowSyncIntent` the app root handles (Settings doesn't import the sync feature), and from the banner's Review. It shows: no account → what that means and Sign in…; otherwise the state line, "Last synced 4 min ago · 2 changes waiting", Sync now, and each conflict as a card (task · detail, this device / other device, Keep mine / Keep theirs) with "the other device's change is in use until you choose" (04 §4: the server value is the default; nothing is lost silently). The banner sits above every screen in the shell, only for conflicts (with Review) or when offline, never for local-only use. `connectivity_plus` (already a dependency) goes with the sync engine in M6.3, so offline is the engine's to report. Notifications denied: a notice above Settings › REMINDERS and at the top of the Alerts editor, with Open settings. Where the app can't open them, a snackbar says where to go. Settings re-reads the permission after the button.
- [x] M3f.9 System states on every screen (§5.4): skeleton dots on a dotted thread (no spinner > 300 ms), empty, inline actionable error, offline, `DatabaseUnavailable` recovery screen (M2.4: explain, move the file aside, never delete, start fresh, point to sync / import; on web `openInAnotherTab` says "Dottoday is open in another tab" with "Use it here" — today that tab is blank, seen in the M3d.4 sweep), reduced motion, 200 % text, RTL, VoiceOver/TalkBack labels (R-12). Ref derived — §5.3/5.4. Done: §7 for every screen × state; a test that each screen has all states.
  - [x] M3f.9a Database recovery (split from M3f.9). Notes: `DatabaseStatus` (data/db) opens the database with a first query, and `DatabaseGate` at the app root shows the recovery screen instead of every screen while it can't be used. Missing or rejected key: what happened, then Start fresh. It asks once more inline (the screen sits above the navigator, so no dialogs). It renames `dottoday.db` (+ -wal / -shm) to `….unreadable-<time>`, never deleting it. On the web it moves to the next IndexedDB generation and the old database stays. It then shows where the old file is kept, and Continue restarts the app. `AppRestart` (core/app) owns the provider container and replaces it, so nothing keeps the old error. Open in another tab (web): "Dottoday is open in another tab · Use it here". This tab asks the other over a BroadcastChannel; that tab closes the database (flushing to IndexedDB), releases its Web Lock and says "Dottoday moved to another tab", with its own Use it here. This tab then reloads. If the other tab doesn't answer within 5 s: "Close it and try again". Checked in headless Chrome with two tabs: the second showed the screen, took over, and opened Today; the first showed "moved". Sign-in and import as ways back arrive with M6.1 / M8.1; the screen names them.
  - [x] M3f.9b Loading (skeleton dots, no spinner > 300 ms), inline actionable error, empty, offline on every screen, plus a test that each screen has all states; 200 % text, RTL and reduced motion checked per screen. Notes: `shared/states`: `LoadingThread` (still skeleton dots on a dotted thread, so nothing to reduce for reduced motion, and clipped in a short slot) and `ErrorState` (what failed, "your tasks are safe on this device", Try again; a failed load says "couldn't read", never "couldn't save"). `waitFor` / `StateScaffold` / `screenState` turn a screen's async inputs into one of them; a value that has loaded before keeps showing through a refresh error. Used by Today (and each day page), Inbox, Week, Month, Settings, Search's results and the task page; an id that isn't the user's still reads "not here" (06 §9). Offline is the M3f.8 banner, above every screen. `test/screen_states_test.dart`: every screen × loading (skeleton, no spinner), error (inline, Try again reloads), empty (its own text, no skeleton), and demo data at 200 % text, right-to-left and reduced motion, compact and expanded. It found and fixed: Today's date caption, the wide Inbox header (actions now wrap under the title), the week summary chips at 390 px (even at 100 %), and the week header at 200 % (it gets half the height and scrolls itself). Before this, every screen showed a blank page while loading and forever on an error.
- [x] M3f.9c (was M3f.9b; renamed when M3f.9 split into 9a/9b) Inbox reorder (drag handle, `sortOrder`) and bulk select (reuses M3b.7's selection bar) — PRD F3 lists both; M3b.8 shipped without them. Done: §7 + reorder persistence test. Notes: the Inbox was already stored in `sortOrder` order, so "Sort: added" is now the user's own order. Each row has a drag handle, and Move up / Move down for keyboards and screen readers; a move renumbers the shown rows 1…n in one undoable batch (domain `reorderInbox`). Handles show only in that order with no filter, and not while picking; on Mac/web the handle reorders while the rest of the row still drags onto the thread. Bulk select: the row menu (long-press on a phone, right-click on Mac/web) and a screen-reader action have Select; a tap then picks or unpicks, and the row shows a check instead of "+". The M3b.7 bar's frame, colour sheet, delete confirm and count announcement moved to `shared/selection`, so Today and the Inbox share one bar. Inbox actions: Plan these (each where it first fits this week, in list order, like Auto-plan), Colour, Delete (asks first). Each is one write with Undo and ends selecting; × or Escape ends it too. Also fixed: the swipe hint at 200 % text.
- [x] M3f.10 `/ship-check` + the full screenshot sweep + four-platform side-by-side diff. Done 2026-10-22: `dart analyze --fatal-infos` and format clean; 1,874 tests (1,870 + 4 perf); coverage `lib/domain` 97.2 %, overall 94.0 % (hand-written); launch integration test green on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator and the Android API 37 emulator; security §9 walked (no schema, dependency or CSP change; no new log calls; the only localStorage use is the web database generation number; account deletion is a typed confirm, still UI-only until M6.5; the deep-link allow-list takes exactly the six Settings pages; an unknown task id still reads "not here"). Sweep: the device sweep (`sweep_test.dart`, now also Settings › Account, the Sync sheet, Running late and Inbox selecting) on iPhone, Android and Mac (`tool/mac_sweep.sh`), and Chrome at 1280 and 390 px in light and dark over every route, the six Settings pages included. Fixed from it: on iPhone, sheets opened from a tab (Running late) slid under the floating tab bar, so `showAppSheet` now always uses the root navigator. Only the §4 idioms differ between platforms (tab bar / navigation bar / rail, sheet vs popover, menu corners). Open: `docs/parity-checklist.md` and the 08 §6 manual gates (a11y pass on devices, offline / two-device scenarios, notification exactness, crash-free) belong to the release milestones (M5/M6); headless Chrome still keeps ⌘/ for itself.

## Milestone 4 — Onboarding (goal: first run to a planned first thread in < 90 s, skippable, on all four targets)

Ref for all of M4: derived — §5.3/5.4 (§5.1 has no renders). The old M4 "Capture" tasks are delivered by M3b–M3f.

**Decided 2026-10-21 (owner), PRD F10 updated:** keep O1–O6. Only the three input screens (O2 day shape, O3 habits, O5 account) count against F10's limit, and calendar connect is an optional row on O4.

- [x] M4.1 O1 Welcome: the dot draws itself ("Your day, as a thread."), Get started / Sign in; reduced motion = static. Done: §7. Notes: `OnboardingGate` at the app root shows the flow until it is finished on this device. Progress (step + finished) is kept in `local_meta`, never synced, so a killed flow resumes and a new device asks again; if the record can't be read, the app opens anyway. The flow has its own navigator (it sits above the app's), and a frame with Back, step dots read as "Step 2 of 6", and Skip on every step after the welcome. O1: the day dot fills once (`DayDot`'s own animation, so with reduced motion it is simply full), "Your day, as a thread.", Get started, and "I already have an account" (to O5). Steps not built yet only offer Continue, so nobody is stuck while M4.2–M4.6 land. Also fixed: text buttons now use the accent's ink theme-wide (the raw accent was 4.3:1 on the page, under 4.5:1). Integration tests pass onboarding with `pass_onboarding.dart`, and wait for the ⋯ menu (now five items) to finish opening before tapping.
- [x] M4.2 O2 Day shape: "When does your day start and end?", Wake Up / Bedtime wheels + live mini-thread (the Settings DAY SHAPE component). Done: §7 + writes the profile through `SettingsRepository`. Notes: the Settings sun → moon slider (its live mini-thread is the Wake Up → Bedtime bar with "15 h awake") in a raised card. Each move saves at once through `updateSettings` → `SettingsRepository`, setting the everyday window like Settings does, so weekdays with their own window keep it. Skip keeps the defaults (07:00–22:00). The wheels the plan names are the slider's two handles; exact times and per-weekday windows stay in Settings, and the text says so. `StepFrame` is the shared layout for the steps that ask something.
- [x] M4.3 O3 Habits (skippable): pick 3–5 suggestion chips via `ColourIconBlock` + suggestion rows; they land on tomorrow's thread. Done: §7 + a test that they land on tomorrow, not today. Notes: seven starters (Morning run, Deep work, Lunch, Walk, Gym, Read, Plan tomorrow), each a suggestion row (dot, title, "07:30 · 30 min") with a tick; up to five (then "Five is plenty to start with." and the rest are off). Times follow the user's own day, which a fixed clock time wouldn't: morning ones after tomorrow's Wake Up, evening ones end before Bedtime, Lunch / Walk / Gym at their usual hour (domain `StarterHabit.startOn`). "Add 3 to tomorrow" writes them as planned one-off tasks in one batch (one Undo); they are the user's to make repeat later. Skip or nothing picked → Continue; a failed write stays on the step and says so.
- [x] M4.4 O4 Reminders: explain task-start and Bedtime reminders, then the system permission prompt; optional "Connect a calendar" row (device calendar permission, M7.1 picks the calendars); denied → the M3f.8 state. Done: §7 + test both outcomes. Notes: the two kinds as rows ("Before each task", how early is set in Settings; "At Bedtime" with the Bedtime reminder switch, on by default and saved at once), then Allow notifications (the system prompt, through `NotificationPermission`) or Not now (asks nothing). Allowed → on; denied → the M3f.8 "Notifications are off" notice, then Continue. Until the scheduler lands (M5.1) the stand-in always says yes. Deferred to M7.1: the "Connect a calendar" row is shown but off ("Calendar import arrives in a later update"). Asking for the device calendar permission needs native permission strings (Info.plist usage text, Android manifest), which is native code and a must-ask item, and M7.1 is where calendars are picked anyway.
- [x] M4.5 O5 Account: Apple / Google / email magic link / "Continue without an account" (UI against the auth interface; real auth in M6.1). Done: §7. Notes: "Continue without an account" is the main button, because the app is whole without one (02 F7). Apple / Google / email are outlined buttons calling `AccountService.signIn`: off with "Sign-in arrives with sync" until M6.1, and with a working service (tested) success moves on while a failure stays and says why. Email's address field and "check your mail" are M4.7's states. "I already have an account" on O1 lands here.
- [x] M4.6 O6 Done: the first thread with the habits placed, "Open today". Done: §7. Notes: tomorrow on the shared `DayCard` (Wake Up → Bedtime, "0 of 3 · 1 h 45 planned", the habits from O3 with the free gaps between) from a data-layer `firstThread` provider (onboarding doesn't import the timeline feature). With nothing picked: "Tomorrow … is all yours". "Open today" finishes onboarding and the app opens on Today. All six steps are now built.
- [x] M4.7 Auth states: returning-user sign-in, magic-link "check your mail", account error, restore-from-sync progress. Done: §7 + a widget test per state. Notes: `AccountService` gains `sendMagicLink(email)` and `watchRestore()` (`RestoreProgress` done/total), and the domain gains `checkEmail` (required, ≤ 254, one @ with a dotted domain; deliverability is the mail server's call). O5 is a small state machine. Choose: Apple / Google / email. Email: the address field (email keyboard and autofill) and "Send me a link"; a bad address says so, asks nothing. Check your mail: the address, Send it again, Use a different email. Error: "Couldn't sign in", the reason, and Try again, which repeats the last action. Restoring: after signing in, "Bringing your tasks to this device… 120 of 300" with a filling bar (never a spinner), then on to O6. Returning user: O1's "I already have an account" is kept in the onboarding progress, and O5 then says "Welcome back · Sign in to bring your tasks to this device". All against fakes; M6.1 wires the real Supabase flows.
- [x] M4.8 Flow rules: at most 3 input screens (PRD F10), every step skippable, "Continue without an account" path, < 90 s timed in an integration test, resumes if killed mid-flow. Done: integration test. Notes: `OnboardingStep.asksForInput` marks O2, O3 and O5, and a test holds it to ≤ 3. A widget test skips every step from O2 to O6 and takes the no-account path from the welcome to the app. `integration_test/onboarding_test.dart` runs the real app on a device. It resets onboarding, goes Get started → O2 → O3, then kills the app half-way (AppRestart replaces the provider container) and checks it comes back on O3. It then picks Lunch, Add 1, Not now, Continue without an account, Open today → Today, timed under 90 s. It ran green in 9 s on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator and 13 s on the Android API 37 emulator.
- [x] M4.9 Integration test onboarding → quick add → drag with shift → recurrence edit scopes; add the Android-emulator `integration_test` CI job (08 §6). `/ship-check` + four-platform side-by-side diff. Done 2026-10-22. `integration_test/journey_test.dart` runs onboarding → quick add ("Journey check 9:30pm 15m") → drag the standup onto the next gap with Shift later tasks on (a second finger on the chip) → edit the repeating standup's title for All. It is green on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator and the Android API 37 emulator. It found two real bugs, both fixed:
  1. The app's undo service refused any write that saves a series, so starting a repeat and every "this and following" / "all" edit or delete threw. The M3f.1 tests had used a fake that accepted them. `InMemoryUndoService` now remembers the series and all their occurrences, and Undo restores them and removes what the write materialised (real-database tests added).
  2. On iPhone, the task sheet, task editor, Inbox schedule sheet and colour sheet opened under the floating tab bar, which covered the editor's Save. They now open on the root navigator.

  CI: a new `integration-android` job runs the launch, onboarding and journey tests on an API 34 emulator (android-emulator-runner pinned by SHA). Ship-check: analyze and format clean; 1,965 tests (with coverage; perf separate); coverage `lib/domain` 96.9 %, overall 94.0 %; security §9 walked: no schema, dependency or CSP change; onboarding progress is a device-only `local_meta` row with no task content; the sign-in email is validated on the client (the server is M6.1); no new log calls; account actions are UI-only. Sweep: the device sweep now walks O1–O6 with a shot per step (Lunch picked) before Today, on iPhone, Android and Mac; Chrome at 1280 and 390 px, light and dark, captures every onboarding step and every route. Only the §4 idioms differ. Debug-only artifact seen: a device that loaded the demo day on two different days shows two standups ahead (each load makes its own daily series); not product data.

## Milestone 5 — Notifications, widgets, platform polish

- [x] M5.0 Register `dottoday://` and universal/app links (iOS associated domains, Android App Links + intent filter, macOS URL scheme); verify a cold-start deep link to `/task/:id` opens the task on each platform. Router allow-list from M0.5. (07 shared) Done 2026-10-23: `dottoday://` registered on iOS (`CFBundleURLTypes`, `FlutterDeepLinkingEnabled`), Android (VIEW/BROWSABLE intent filter, `flutter_deeplinking_enabled`) and macOS (`CFBundleURLTypes`). Cold start → `/task/:id` opened the right demo task on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator, the Android API 37 emulator and the Mac (warm links too); `integration_test/deep_link_test.dart` covers the pushed link with a real task. Notes: the router receives the whole link (scheme and host) and `allowListedLocation` canonicalises it, so no Dart parsing was added. Two native gaps needed a bridge (`dottoday/deep_links`, `core/router/native_deep_links.dart`): the macOS embedder never forwards opened URLs, and on an iOS cold start the engine's push can beat the router and is dropped ("Failed to handle route information"). The Apple runners queue launch links and Dart takes them when it starts listening. Rejected: `app_links` (a new dependency for two small shims). Universal links / App Links are deferred by the owner's call (they need a domain and the paid Apple account): templates and steps in `app/tool/well_known/`, tracked on the store-readiness checklist.
- [x] M5.1 `NotificationScheduler` diffing against OS pending set; IDs from `hash(occurrenceId, alertIndex)`; actions Done/Snooze/Open; channels (Android), interruption levels (iOS); exactness test 20/20/0 duplicates on iOS+Android. (04 §6, R-10) Done 2026-10-23: `integration_test/notification_exactness_test.dart` — 20 alerts through the real write path, re-synced three times: **iOS (iPhone 17 Pro sim) scheduled 20 / fired 20 / 0 duplicates / 0 pending after; Android (API 37 emulator, exact alarms) 20 / 20 / 0 / 0.** Notes: `OsNotificationGateway` (data) over a `NotificationPlatform` port, `FlnNotificationPlatform` on flutter_local_notifications for iOS/Android/macOS; web stays unwired until M5.3. The payload round-trips the `PlannedNotification` (IDs and timing only, 06 §1) plus a 12-hex hash of the shown text, so the planner's diff sees exactly what the OS holds and a renamed task is re-posted; an unreadable payload reads as stale and is replaced. Android channels Task alerts (high) and Bedtime; iOS interruption levels passive (quiet hours) / time-sensitive (at start) / active. Actions Done / Snooze 10 min / Open bring the app forward and are handled in the main isolate (`NotificationResponder`, through the write path). Rejected: a background isolate for actions (a second encrypted DB connection; revisit with M8 hardening if Done-without-opening is asked for). Snooze only re-reminds (PRD F6 clarified). `FlnNotificationPermission` keeps an "asked" flag in `local_meta` (iOS/Android 13 can't tell never-asked from denied); Android has no settings deep link, so the M3f.8 notice says where to go. O4's copy is the owner's text. Exact alarms are used when Android grants them; the rationale/ask is M5.6. The time-sensitive entitlement isn't added (Personal Team), so iOS treats those alerts as active — on the store-readiness list.
- [x] M5.2 Background refresh: iOS BGAppRefresh, Android WorkManager — re-materialise + re-plan daily. (07) Done 2026-10-23 (ADR-0008, `workmanager` ^0.10 approved by the owner). Found on the way: series were only materialised when saved, so a repeat ran out 90 days after its last edit; `DailyRefresh` now rolls every due series forward (re-saved through the write path) and re-plans notifications, on start, resume, every 6 h while open, and from WorkManager (Android, 24 h) / BGAppRefresh (iOS, `dottoday.refresh`). Verified: `test/data/refresh/daily_refresh_test.dart` (30 days on → window +30 d; second run same day writes nothing); `integration_test/background_refresh_test.dart` on the Android API 37 emulator — the worker in its own isolate rolled a run-out repeat to +90 d. iOS BGTaskScheduler doesn't run on the simulator: build + registration only here; a device check is added to M8.5.
- M5.3 Web push: service worker, VAPID, `send-push` Edge Function; in-app fallback. (07 Web) Split in M5.3 (CLAUDE.md §10): pushes need an account, which is wired in M6.1.
  - [x] M5.3a Reminders on the web while the tab is open + in-app fallback. Done 2026-10-23: `TimerNotificationPlatform` (one `Timer` per pending reminder, behind the same `OsNotificationGateway`, so plan/diff/payload are shared with the other platforms) posts through `web/sw.js` (`registration.showNotification`, Done + Open — Chrome shows two actions) or, without permission or in quiet hours, raises `InAppAlertBanner` above every screen (Done/Open, Later). Taps come back from the worker by `postMessage` (queued if it had to open the page) into the same `NotificationResponder`. `WebNotificationPermission` maps `default`/`granted`/`denied`. Flutter's own service worker is switched off (custom `web/flutter_bootstrap.js`: it unregisters itself and would take `sw.js`, same scope, with it); `sw.js` is registered from `register_sw.js`, not inline, so the CSP can stay `script-src 'self'` (M5.7). Verified in the built-in browser (permission denied there): a task's 10-min reminder raised the banner on time and Done ticked it. The built-in browser blocks service workers, so posting through `sw.js` in real Chrome is a manual release gate (08 §6). Reused: the banner strip is now `shared/feedback/banner_strip.dart` (sync banner too).

Live delivery to a browser push service needs the owner's VAPID secrets + deploy (cron snippet in `supabase/snippets/`). `push_subscriptions` / `scheduled_pushes` migration with RLS, payloads encrypted in the browser to the subscription (RFC 8291; the server stores ciphertext + fire time only, 06 §1), `send-push` Edge Function (VAPID ES256), cron snippet for the owner's deploy. Wired on sign-in (M6.1); tested against local Supabase.

Notes: the Dart side writes a display snapshot (`data/widgets/widget_display.dart`: times on the profile clock, localised labels, resolved colours, deep links by id) after every write and on the daily refresh (a quiet day still moves the 24 h window, so `DailyRefresh` now redraws too), so the native widgets only lay out. Android: Glance (Compose compiler plugin added to the app module; no new package) — Next up 2×2, Today 4×2 (rows fit the size), Inbox 2×2; taps open `dottoday://task/<id>` / `today` / `inbox`; redraws scheduled at each start/end via `HomeWidget.scheduleWidgetUpdates`. iOS: WidgetKit extension `DottodayWidgets` (added by `app/tool/ios_add_widget_target.rb`, reproducible) — Next up (also Lock Screen `accessoryRectangular`, 07 iOS), Today (medium/large), Inbox; timeline entries at every start/end; app group `group.app.dottoday.planner`. Verified: all three on the Android API 37 emulator (render, tap → task, ticking the next task moves Next up on); on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator the extension reads the snapshot (Next up placed on the home screen). Found: `DateFormat` outside the widget tree (background refresh, notification text) had no locale data → `initializeDateFormatting()` in `bootstrap()` and the background entry. Decisions: macOS gets "Next up" in the menu bar (PRD F9 — the more specific doc), built in M5.5, not a WidgetKit widget; App Groups can't be signed by the Personal Team, so iOS widgets on a device wait for the paid account (simulator works) — on the store-readiness list. Widget picker names (Android strings.xml, Swift display names) are English until M8.3.

Keys a text field also uses (⌘Z, ⌘←/⌘→) are listed without native key equivalents — a menu equivalent takes the key before Flutter and would break typing; Flutter's bindings keep them (⌘Z at the root = Edit › Undo when no field has focus, via the new `UndoService.latest`; Go items reach Today through `ShowDayIntent`/`ShowTodayIntent`). Menu-bar "Next up" (PRD F9): `NSStatusItem` fed the widget snapshot over `dottoday/status_item` (`MacStatusItemSink`), redraws itself at each start/end, lists what is left today, opens a task through the M5.0 link bridge; ⌘-drag removes it. Window: 1280×800, min 960×640 (owner-approved; 07 said 900×600). Sandbox: user-selected read/write (export) on both, network client on Release (= M6.1b). App name shows as "Dottoday" on Mac, iPhone and Android (was "dottoday"). Verified: release build is universal (x86_64 + arm64); the x86_64 slice ran 90 s under Rosetta with no error or crash report; the arm64 build runs; menu bar and "● Team standup · 8:00 AM" captured; the rail sits below the standard title bar, clear of the traffic lights. Predictive back: `android:enableOnBackInvokedCallback="true"` + `PredictiveBackPageTransitionsBuilder` for Android in the theme (iOS/macOS keep Cupertino transitions). Edge-to-edge: `SystemUiMode.edgeToEdge` at start (screens already sit in SafeArea). Exact alarms: `ExactAlarms` port (`FlnExactAlarms` on Android, always allowed elsewhere); when Android 12+ doesn't allow them, Settings › Reminders shows "Reminders may come a few minutes late" with the rationale and "Allow exact timing" (opens Alarms & reminders); the permission and exact state are re-read on every resume; the platform re-checks on each resync and, when the answer changes, cancels and re-posts every alert so existing ones switch between inexact and exact (reminders keep coming inexact meanwhile). Verified on the API 37 emulator: denied → notice → Allow opens Alarms & reminders → switched on → back in the app the notice is gone; edge-to-edge Settings and Today screenshots. CI's web job builds with the CSP and runs axe, the encrypted-DB probe and `tool/a11y/pwa_check.mjs` (no CSP violation, worker controls the page, opens offline after one visit, offline.html on a cold offline visit) under it. Keyboard: a "Skip to content" link first on Tab (`shared/navigation/skip_link.dart`); screens that focus their main control on open already start past the navigation; the rest of the keyboard map is M3e.6. 360 px: `test/responsive_360_test.dart` (Today, Inbox, Search, Settings). Verified in the built-in browser: app runs under the CSP with no violation; service-worker parts run in CI (the built-in browser blocks workers). Hosting the headers is the owner's deploy step (readiness list).

- [x] M5.9 Time-zone change banner (keep vs shift). (07 shared) Done 2026-10-23: the device zone is re-read on resume; when it differs from the profile's, a strip above every screen says "You're now in New York — keep times or shift?". Keep times (first, the 07 default) moves every planned task that hasn't started to the same clock time in the new zone and splits each repeat at its next instance (`SeriesChange.tzId`, same path as a new time, no duplicates); Shift keeps the moments and only changes the profile's zone. Started, done, skipped, inbox and calendar items are left alone. Both are one write with Undo (the undo service now puts a batch's profile back — it used to refuse, found on the emulator). On a phone a two-action strip puts its actions under the text (also the in-app reminder). Verified on the Android emulator (Berlin → New York: keep, undo, shift).

## Milestone 6 — Accounts & sync

- [x] M6.0 `supabase db push` to the linked project, then verify the RLS tests against it. (CLAUDE.md §7) Done 2026-10-23 at the owner's request: `0001`–`0003` pushed to `pmwgtwosihtcvfrzvobu`; `tool/hosted_rls_test.sh` ran the pgTAP suite against it, 32/32. Hosted pgTAP lives in `extensions`, which the CLI's temporary login role can't use, so the script runs copies that `set local role postgres` and add `extensions` to the path inside each test's own transaction (rolled back — nothing is left on the hosted database).
- [x] M6.1 Auth (screens built in M4.5/M4.7, wired here): magic link, Sign in with Apple, Google (PKCE); session in secure storage; "sign out everywhere". App remains fully usable without an account. (02 F7, 06 §2) Also from M5.3b: on the web, when signed in and notifications are allowed, subscribe to push (`browserPushSubscription` with `WEB_PUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`), register it (`SupabasePushStore.subscribe`) and wrap the web platform in `ServerPushMirror`; integration-test the store against local Supabase. Done 2026-10-23 (code + local; hosted steps on the readiness list). `SupabaseAccountService` over Supabase Auth with PKCE for every method: Apple and Google open the provider's page (`signInWithOAuth`, no native SDK, so no new dependency), email sends a one-time link (`signInWithOtp`); Sign out (this device) and **Sign out everywhere** (global scope). Session and PKCE verifier live in an `AuthVault`: Keychain/Keystore via `flutter_secure_storage` on devices; on the web `localStorage` sealed with its own non-extractable AES-GCM key in the key vault (ADR-0003; a CI-tagged web test checks the raw value isn't the session). The apps get the code at `dottoday://auth-callback`, caught by the router before the allow-list on every platform and handed to the service (an inbox holds cold-start links); the web lets Supabase read `?code=` from its own address. Without a Supabase client (tests, or start failed) the app stays on `LocalOnlyAccountService`. Verified against local Supabase: a tagged test (`test/supabase/`, new `client` job in the supabase workflow) runs the email link through Mailpit, the server's redirect, the code exchange and the web-push store under RLS; on the iPhone simulator (Mail link → Safari → "Open in Dottoday" → signed in → kept across relaunch → Sign out everywhere = server `/logout`), on the Android emulator (callback delivered as the browser would) and in Chrome (sealed session, `?code=` consumed). Fixed on the way: during onboarding the app (and its router) wasn't mounted, so iOS dropped the sign-in link opened from the mail — the app now stays mounted, hidden, under the flow; O5 moved on when a provider's page merely opened (closing it skipped ahead) — it now waits for the account; "I already have an account" never set the returning flag ("Welcome back"); Settings › Account's email button had no address step (O5's pieces moved to `shared/account`); the page no longer shows "Sign in" while the session loads. Web push: when signed in with notifications allowed and a VAPID key, the tab's reminders are mirrored to `scheduled_pushes` (`withServerPush`); it never asks for permission itself. Apple/Google need the owner's consoles (Services ID, OAuth client, redirect URLs) — readiness list.
- [x] M6.1b macOS `Release.entitlements` lacks `com.apple.security.network.client` (found in M3b.9; DebugProfile got it for the VM service) → add it with the first hosted-Supabase build, or sync is blocked by the sandbox. Done early in M5.5.
- [x] M6.2 `SupabaseGateway` + `SyncEngine`: push via `sync_push`, Realtime subscription, periodic `since` pull, HLC, tombstones, server-error-never-deletes-local; tests from 08 §2 `sync_engine_test`. (04 §4, ADR-0002, R-08) Done 2026-10-23. `SyncEngine` (pull with per-field merge, then push the outbox in batches of 500, parents first) behind a `SyncGateway` port: `SupabaseSyncGateway` (`sync_push`, PostgREST pulls and `field_versions`, Realtime nudges) and an in-memory `FakeSyncServer` faithful to `sync_push` for tests. `EngineSyncService` drives it: idle while signed out; on sign-in adopts the account (device profile re-keyed, rows follow) and syncs; then every 5 min, ~3 s after local writes, on Realtime nudges, on resume and on Sync now; status synced/syncing/offline/error with last sync, pending count and conflicts; "keep mine" writes the value again through the write path. Tests (08 §2 `sync_engine_test`): push/pull, offline and server errors keep the outbox and local rows, LWW both ways, conflict within 60 s (server value stands, "mine" kept), different fields both survive, tombstones, pages, adoption with pre-existing rows; and a tagged round trip on local Supabase with two clients of one account (every column type crosses, repeat instances identical, an edit reaches the other device). Found by the tests and fixed: a device saw its own earlier write as a conflict (same-node edits now never conflict); a field edited on top of the version already pulled looked like a conflict (new local table `field_seen`, schema 2, migration tested); repeat instances had random ids per device (now `instanceId`, deterministic). ADR-0002 amended with the engine rules. Not covered: background (OS) refresh doesn't sync yet — the next foreground resume does (moved to Milestone 6.5: the `workmanager` job that re-arms alarms also syncs, roadmap A0).
- [x] M6.3 Sync status pill + details screen (UI from M3f.8, wired here) (conflicts list with keep mine/theirs). (R-08) Done 2026-10-23. The M3f.8 Sync sheet, banner and conflict cards now read the real `EngineSyncService` (state, "Last synced 4 min ago", pending changes, Sync now, Keep mine / Keep theirs). Concept A shows status as text, not the 03 §3.1 pill: "Kody · synced just now" in the Settings header (`A-ios-settings`, `A-android-settings`) and "● Synced · just now" at the foot of the Inbox sidebar (`A-mac-week-alt3-board`) — one shared `SyncStatusLine` (synced/syncing/offline/paused/needs you; the text carries the state, the dot repeats it). The unused `SyncPill` is replaced by it and removed (10 wins over 03). Golden for the line's states in both themes; widget test for the header.
- [x] M6.4 First-sync merge for users who created local data before signing in (local wins, server rows merged by id). Done 2026-10-23. An entity's first pull for an account lets pending local writes win (`localWins`), and where the server holds a newer clock for such a field the outbox row is re-stamped to outrank it (`HybridLogicalClock.outrank`, also past the 5-min drift bound, since the server already accepted that version) — so local wins on the server too, not just on the device. Rows only on the device go up; rows only on the server come down; same id → per field as above. Spec clarification (04 §4): the profile this device made on first launch holds defaults, not choices — where another device already wrote the account's profile, those initial values (same clock as the profile's `created_at`) give way, while anything changed here before signing in still wins; so a returning user's new phone keeps their settings. O5's "Bringing your tasks… 120 of 300" now counts the first sync (`SyncService.watchRestore`, from the server's own row counts), and every run ends it, so O5 never waits on a device that is offline. Tests: fake server (returning account keeps settings; a pre-sign-in choice wins; a pre-sign-in row wins on the server; progress 0 → all, later runs silent) and the local-Supabase round trip (progress from the server's counts).
- [x] M6.5 Account deletion Edge Function + settings flow + email confirmation; export before delete prompt. (06 §6) Done 2026-10-23. `delete-account` Edge Function: takes the caller from their own session (never an id from the body; the gateway verifies the JWT), deletes the auth user with the service role — which only this function holds — so `profiles` and every row cascade at once (well inside "within 24 h"), then mails the confirmation through Resend when `RESEND_API_KEY` + `ACCOUNT_MAIL_FROM` are set (a failed mail never undoes the deletion; without them it logs and the owner's checklist says so). Node tests for the logic; a tagged local-Supabase test signs in, syncs a task, deletes, and checks with the local stack's admin key that the user's rows are gone while the device keeps its copy and is signed out. Settings › Account › Delete account keeps the typed DELETE and adds "Export my data first" (to Settings › Export; the formats arrive in M8.1). If this device later signs into an account — any other than the one it last synced with — everything on it is queued again (`requeueAll`), since acknowledged rows had left the outbox. Owner steps: deploy the function, set the two mail secrets (readiness list).
- [x] M6.6 Two-device integration test (two app instances against local Supabase): offline edit both, reconnect, no loss, conflict surfaced only for same-field-within-60 s. Done 2026-10-23. `test/supabase/two_devices_test.dart` (CI: supabase workflow, client job): two devices of one account on local Supabase, each with its own clock, edit while offline and reconnect — different fields both survive (no conflict); the same field within a minute raises one conflict on the device that syncs second, the server value stands and the device's text waits in the conflict for "keep mine"; the same field minutes apart: the later edit wins everywhere, no conflict; new tasks on both arrive on both; deleted on one + edited on the other = a tombstone in Trash that kept the edit. Also by hand with two app instances (iPhone 17 Pro simulator + Android emulator, one account, local Supabase): a task added to the Android Inbox reached the iPhone's Inbox within seconds; placing it on the iPhone put it on Android's Today at 11:45. Found and fixed: O5 showed the sign-in choices to a device that was already signed in (it now starts at "bringing your tasks"). Known limit (ADR-0002): a device whose clock runs more than 5 min fast is refused by `sync_push` and shows "Sync paused" until its clock is right — moved to Milestone 6.5: the notification-health panel says the clock is off instead of pausing silently (roadmap A0).
- [x] M6.7 Sentry wiring with PII scrubbing test. (04 §9) Done 2026-10-23. Sentry starts only when `SENTRY_DSN` is set (`sendDefaultPii` off, no screenshots or view hierarchy — they show titles — no automatic UI or native breadcrumbs). Every error reaches it through the PII-checked Logger: `SentryLogSink` turns records into breadcrumbs (fields already safe) and errors into a `LoggedError` that carries the error's type and the log event, never its message; Sentry's own Dart error handlers are replaced by ours again after init, so there is one path. `beforeSend` is the second line: it drops user, request, server and device names, every exception message except our `LoggedError`, foreign breadcrumbs, and any message that isn't ours. Tests: the scrubber on an event full of PII, and a canary — an error whose message holds a task title arrives as "StateError at editor.save_failed". Web: `tool/web_csp.sh` takes the DSN and allows only its ingest host. Owner steps: create the Sentry project, put the DSN in `.env.prod` (readiness list). Note: native crash reports (iOS/Android SDKs) don't pass the Dart `beforeSend`; they hold no app text but may carry the device model — acceptable for crash-free tracking (06 §6), reviewed again at M8.
- [x] M6.8 `/ship-check` incl. RLS tests and security checklist. Done 2026-10-23 (everything but M6.0, the owner's `db push`). `dart analyze --fatal-infos` and format clean; 2,154 tests; coverage `lib/domain` 97.0 %, overall 90.9 % (`data/sync` 79.6 % in the plain run — the rest is exercised by the `supabase`-tagged tests); `supabase db reset` + `supabase test db` 32/32 and the 8 tagged local-Supabase client tests (auth flow, round trip, two devices, delete account, web push store) green locally and in CI; integration tests 7/7 on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator and 7/7 on the Android emulator; CI app/supabase/security green. Security §9: no new table or migration in M6.2–M6.7 (`field_seen` is a local Drift table, schema 2); gitleaks clean in CI; new inputs — the sign-in email (client `checkEmail`, GoTrue on the server), auth callbacks (exact scheme/host, PKCE code only), pulled rows (allow-listed columns, server HLC bound) — validated both sides; no task content in logs (the PII policy test covers every new call; sync logs ids and literal events), crash reports (`scrubEvent` canary) or push payloads (ciphertext only, M5.3b); the session and PKCE verifier live in the Keychain/Keystore, and on the web sealed with a non-extractable key (tested), a reinstall on Apple platforms now starts signed out; sign out everywhere and delete account are confirmed (typed DELETE), deletion was checked end to end on local Supabase, export is still M8.1; no dependency added (sentry_flutter was already in the stack); deep links: the callback is caught before the allow-list and carries only a code; CSP: unchanged unless a Sentry DSN is passed, which adds just its ingest host. Sweep: the device sweep on iPhone, Android and Mac (PASS on all three), and Chrome at 1280 and 390 px in light and dark through Account (signed out, email, check your mail, signed in), the Settings header and the Inbox sidebar's sync line, with a real sign-in to local Supabase in both onboarding and Settings. Only the §4 idioms differ between platforms. Fixed from it: after signing in during onboarding, Settings and the Inbox stayed on "On this device" — the app under the flow is paused by Riverpod and the status stream's `async*` lost the change; `watch`/`watchRestore` now subscribe at once (test fails without the fix). Release gates (08 §6) still open, for M8.5: the owner's hosted steps (M6.0, function deploys, secrets, consoles), the manual hardware passes, crash-free over a beta. Carried to Milestone 6.5 (roadmap A0): a device clock more than 5 min fast pauses sync (→ one line in the notification-health panel), OS background refresh doesn't sync (→ the `workmanager` job syncs too). Note for M8: native crash reports skip the Dart scrubber.

## Milestone 6.5 — Delivery guarantees (goal: every dot-start reminder arrives; before M7)

Spec: `11-standout-roadmap.md` A0, B1, B2, F3 (ADR-0008 workmanager, ADR-0009, ADR-0010). Tasks are numbered M6.5.1… (task M6.5 above is account deletion). Built with the `local-notifications-scheduler` rules: the database is the only truth and the OS pending set a cache reconciled through the one `NotificationSync` path; scheduling math stays pure and `Clock`-injected; `flutter_local_notifications` stays in its one adapter; inexact by default with `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` as the opt-in, never `USE_EXACT_ALARM`; ≤ 50 pending on iOS. Deferred, not here: per-task alert tiers (nudge/alert/alarm) and escalation when no `actualStart` arrives (shares its trigger with M10.6, built there once).

**Done for every task in M6.5–M12** (on top of each task's own criteria): CLAUDE.md §5; `10-ui-concept-a.md` §7 (golden vs reference or "derived — §id" golden, light + dark, compact 390 / medium 700 / expanded 1280, a11y, l10n, sweep entry in 08 §7 and `docs/design-references/README.md`); CLAUDE.md §11 rules — **proposal** (model/rule changes go through `ProposalSheet`; a test proves nothing is written before Accept), **surface** (OS surfaces read `DaySnapshot` and call the five intents only, never Supabase), **no-shame** (the `no_shame_copy` policy test passes for every new string). Each feature lists iPhone, Android and Mac/web behaviour; Android and Mac/web derive from the iPhone render per `10-ui-concept-a.md` §4 (same geometry, states, copy; only §4 chrome differs).

- [x] M6.5.1 Schema for B1/B2 (additive only): `occurrences.actual_start_utc`, `actual_end_utc` (nullable ms epoch); `subtasks.duration_min` (nullable, 1–1440) and `sort_index`; `series.duration_from_steps` + `occurrences.duration_from_steps` (bool, default false); template items get `duration_min`. Drift schema 3 with a migration test (pre-migration snapshot, existing rows untouched); `supabase/migrations/0004_actuals_and_steps.sql` (new columns only, existing RLS policies cover them; `sync_push` allow-list extended) + pgTAP; `RowCodec`, sync engine, export bundle (new optional fields, older bundles still import) and validators updated. **Blocks** B1, B2. **Done when** `supabase db reset && supabase test db` green, schema-3 migration test green, hostile-fixture round trip includes the new fields. Hosted push: the owner pre-approved (2026-10-23) `supabase db push` of 0004 and 0005 at their milestone closes, only after the local RLS tests pass, followed by `tool/hosted_rls_test.sh`, reported in the summary. Any later migration (routines, M11.4) is a must-ask again. Done 2026-10-23. Columns as listed; B2's "sort index" is the existing `subtasks.sort_order` (rejected: a second ordering column). Step durations 1–1440 min (a step may be shorter than the 5-min task floor). Drift schema 3 adds them with `ALTER TABLE` (the subtask CHECK is column-level so it survives the add); migration tests for 1→3 and 2→3. `0004_actuals_and_steps.sql` + pgTAP (39/39 locally); `sync_push` needed no change (it resolves column types).
