## Milestone 7 — Calendar import & write-back

- [x] M7.1 (also: onboarding O4's "Connect a calendar" row, deferred from M4.4 — ask the device calendar permission there; needs the native usage strings) Device calendars (iOS/macOS EventKit, Android CalendarContract): permission flows with explain + deep link; per-calendar toggles; colour inheritance; all-day strip. (02 F5, R-02) Notes: iOS and Android through `device_calendar` behind the domain `DeviceCalendarGateway` (`domain/calendar/`); web and macOS get `NoDeviceCalendarGateway` ("arrives in a later update") until M7.4 / M7.1b. Permission: the plugin only says yes/no, so "asked" is kept in `local_meta` (as for notifications): not asked → explain + Connect; denied → the shared `PermissionNotice` (calendar variant) with Open settings (iOS `app-settings:`, else "where to go"); re-read on resume. Usage strings: `NSCalendarsFullAccessUsageDescription` + `NSCalendarsUsageDescription`; Android `READ_CALENDAR` + `WRITE_CALENDAR` (the plugin asks for both; write-back is M7.3). Per-calendar switch: one local `calendar_accounts` row per device calendar the person has switched, device id in `external_account_id` (docs/05); never switched = on (connecting means "show my calendars", R-02) — rejected: off until picked (an extra step for everyone), a new table (more concepts), settings JSON (syncs device-only ids). Colour inheritance: the nearest of the 9 task colours (`inheritColorKey`, redmean RGB), so imported blocks keep theme tokens — rejected: storing the raw ARGB (raw colours in features). All-day: `ExternalEvent` carries an all-day event's dates as UTC midnights (iOS read in the device zone, Android as the plugin returns them); `allDayDates` spreads it over its days (≤ 366) and `allDayStartUtc` + 24 h puts each in the strip (`TimelineDay.isAllDay`); strip chips now open the item to edit. Event links: only http(s) kept (the plugin wraps them in `data:`). M7.2 writes the occurrences from `calendarChoiceListProvider` + `DeviceCalendarGateway.events`. O4: the "Connect a calendar" row asks the OS on tap; allowed → "Connected · choose calendars in Settings"; denied → the calendar notice.
- [x] M7.1b macOS device calendars: `device_calendar` has no macOS implementation, so add a small EventKit method channel in `macos/Runner` (no new package) implementing `DeviceCalendarGateway` (status/request/openSettings → `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_Calendars`, calendars, events), the sandbox entitlement `com.apple.security.personal-information.calendars` and the Info.plist usage strings; switch `deviceCalendarGatewayProvider` on for macOS. Done: same tests as M7.1 against a fake channel, `flutter build macos`, a run on this Mac. (R-02, R-13) Notes: `ChannelDeviceCalendarGateway` (`data/calendar/`) over `dottoday/calendars` ↔ `macos/Runner/CalendarBridge.swift`. Permission comes from `EKEventStore.authorizationStatus` (notDetermined → not asked; macOS 14 write-only counts as denied: it can't read), so no `local_meta` flag here; `requestFullAccessToEvents` on 14+, `requestAccess(to:)` below. All-day events cross as calendar dates (`yyyy-MM-dd` of start and last day in the Mac's calendar) instead of millis — rejected: millis + reading them in the device zone in Dart (the iOS plugin workaround; one more place a zone can go wrong). Errors are `FlutterError` codes only (bad_args, no_calendar, denied), never content; a missing bridge reads as denied. Colours: `NSColor` → sRGB ARGB, then `inheritColorKey` as on iOS. Event ids are `eventIdentifier` (shared by a series' occurrences, as with the plugin on iOS).
- [x] M7.2 Map external events → occurrences (`source=calendar`, `calendar_links`); local-only subtasks/notes on them; links tappable. (R-02, R-09) Notes: `CalendarImporter` (`data/calendar/`) reads the switched-on calendars (`calendarChoiceListProvider` + `DeviceCalendarGateway.events`) from yesterday to 90 days ahead in the device zone and applies `planCalendarImport` (`domain/calendar/`, pure) in one transaction; it runs in housekeeping (start, resume, 6 h), after Connect and after every switch, one run at a time, and does nothing without the permission. Items: a timed event is one item, an all-day event one per day (`allDayStartUtc`, `maxDurationMin`); link key `<event id>@<ms>` (EventKit shares ids across a series' instances), then a leftover single item/link pair of one event id is the same event moved (keeps subtasks, R-09). Validation at the boundary (06 §7): title cleaned and clipped to 200 (empty → "Untitled event"), description + link → notes ≤ 10 000, ids that don't fit skipped, never cut. Each calendar lacking a `calendar_accounts` row gets one (`CalendarSourceRepository.ensure`). Decisions: (1) imports and their subtasks are device-only — the write path upserts them without `change_log` (`isDeviceOnly`), `requeueAll` skips them — rejected: syncing them (every device imports its own calendars, so each would show twice and stale copies would linger). (2) The description arrives as notes — rejected: new `calendar_links` columns (schema change, more concepts). (3) The `etag` holds an import fingerprint (start, length, title/notes hashes) so a re-import applies only what changed in the calendar since; notes edited here are never replaced — rejected: calendar always wins (loses local moves), calendar never wins after import (a moved meeting stays put). (4) A gone event is removed outright unless it carries local work (subtasks, notes edited, done/skipped, started), which goes to Trash detached — rejected: always Trash (switching off Holidays floods it), always delete (loses subtasks). A deleted or purged import keeps its link, so it is not brought back. (5) Imports are written by the importer in its own transaction, validated with `validateBatch`, then `AfterWriteEffects.schedule` — rejected: nesting `WriteUseCase.apply` in the import transaction (its after-write effects then run in the transaction's zone). (6) No Dottoday alerts on imports (`alerts` null; the calendar reminds). Links tappable: `NoteLinks` (sheet and editor) lists up to 5 http(s) links from the notes (`webLinks`, `core/utils/`), each a link row opening the browser through `LinkLauncher`; failure → snackbar. New glyph: link. Golden `task_sheet_links_*` (derived). Web: no device calendars (M7.4); macOS through M7.1b's bridge. Device check: owner gate in 08 §6.1 (M7.7).
- [x] M7.3 Write-back (opt-in per calendar): move/resize/rename → provider; failure banner + retry; etag conflicts. (R-02) Notes: Settings › Calendars › SEND CHANGES BACK lists the switched-on writable calendars, each off until chosen (`calendar_accounts.write_back`, `CalendarSourceRepository.setWriteBack`). Seam for M7.4: `CalendarWriter` (`domain/calendar/calendar_writer.dart`: `read` → `RemoteEvent` with an opaque `version`; `write(patch, expectedVersion)` → `WriteBackOutcome` written / changed / gone / notWritable / failed), picked per `CalendarProvider` in `calendarWriteBackProvider`; Google/Microsoft add a writer whose version is the HTTP etag (If-Match → 412 = changed). Device calendars: `DeviceCalendarWriter` over two new `DeviceCalendarGateway` methods, `event` and `updateEvent`; iOS/Android through the plugin (read the whole event, change start/end/title, save it back, so notes, alarms and attendees go back as read; Android ids must be numeric before they reach the plugin's unquoted query, 06 §7); macOS through the bridge's new `event` / `update` (span `.thisEvent`, refuses repeating/all-day/read-only). Its version is a fingerprint (start, end, title hash, all-day, repeating) compared right before the write. Etag conflicts: `calendar_links.etag` (M7.2's import fingerprint) is the three-way base; `mergeWriteBack` (pure) takes a field changed on one side from that side, so a calendar rename and a local move both survive; the same field changed on both sides to different values is a clash, never overwritten: the banner says "This event changed in your calendar too" with Keep mine / Use calendar's (all clashes at once) — rejected: calendar always wins (silently loses the local move, R-08), Dottoday always wins (silently loses the calendar's change), a per-event review sheet (more UI for a rare case; follow-up if it shows up). After a write the link gets the merged fingerprint and the moved instance's key, so the next import agrees. Only timed, non-repeating events write back (`ImportedItem.writable`, kept in `calendar_links.read_only` with the calendar's own read-only) — rejected: series edits (the plugin saves with `.futureEvents` from the first instance, which would move the whole series), all-day items (one item is one day of a possibly multi-day event). The editor says which: "Changes go back to Work" / "From Holidays · changes stay in Dottoday" (`CalendarWriteLine`, docs/03 §3.4). Trigger: `WriteBackController` watches the pending items (writable link, write-back on, local fields ≠ base) and sends 2 s after the last edit (a typed title sends once); the import flushes it first and leaves still-pending links alone (`heldLinkIds`) so it can't overwrite them. A failure shows "1 change hasn't reached your calendar yet" · Try again; failed or clashing items aren't retried on their own until they change (no retry storm). Gone in the calendar or no longer writable → the link turns read-only, the edit stays here, and the next import removes a gone event. Turning write-back on also sends the moves made before — rejected: freezing earlier edits as local-only (they'd stay different from the calendar forever without saying so). Notes and subtasks never go back. No schema change; no new dependency. Device check: owner gate in 08 §6.1 (M7.7).
- [x] M7.4 Google + Microsoft via Edge Functions (`calendar-oauth`, `calendar-sync`), tokens in Vault; web parity. (04 §7, 06 §1) Notes (ADR-0011): migration `0005_calendar_oauth`: a server `calendar_accounts` row per connected account (unique per user/provider/account id; clients may only read and delete; only the function creates), the refresh/access tokens as JSON in Vault (`calendar_account:<id>`) behind service-role-only functions (`calendar_account_connect`, `calendar_token_get/put`, `calendar_oauth_take_state`), a delete trigger that drops the token, `calendar_oauth_states` (single use, 10 min, PKCE verifier, no client grants); pgTAP `0005_calendar_oauth.sql`. `calendar-oauth`: POST with the session → the provider's URL (PKCE S256, `state`); GET `/callback` swaps the code, names the account from the id token (Google `sub`, Microsoft `oid`; email as label) and returns the browser to `dottoday://settings/calendars` (an existing allowed deep link) or an origin in `CALENDAR_RETURN_ORIGINS` (web, same tab: Drift web runs in one tab); `verify_jwt = false` in config.toml, the JWT is checked in the function. `calendar-sync`: `calendars` / `events` / `event` / `update` (If-Match → 412 = changed) / `disconnect` (Google grant revoked), each calendar id `<account id>/<provider id>` checked against the caller's accounts; access tokens refreshed a minute early, rotated refresh tokens stored, a 401 refreshes once, a refused refresh = `reconnect`. Scopes: Google `calendar.calendarlist.readonly` + `calendar.events`; Microsoft `Calendars.ReadWrite` + `offline_access`. Shared code in `functions/_shared/calendar/` (Deno std + fetch only, no new imports), tested with node in CI (30 tests). App: `FunctionCloudCalendars` (`data/calendar/`) over an `EdgeFunctions` port is a `CalendarEventReader` (the new interface the device gateway also implements) and the `CalendarWriter` for google/microsoft (`writeBackProviders` gains them; version = etag); a second `CalendarImporter` (`cloudCalendarImporterProvider`, `providers: cloudProviderWires`) owns only those links, so neither import removes the other's events; `importEvents` runs device then cloud. Each cloud calendar gets a local `calendar_accounts` row keyed `<account id>/<calendar id>` (`DeviceCalendar.provider`), so switches and write-back opt-ins work as for device calendars. Decisions: (1) read on request, nothing stored server side — rejected: a server `calendar_events` table + sync as 04 §7 sketched (calendar content on our server, a second implementation of the import rules). (2) Cloud imports stay device-only; every signed-in device imports the account itself, so all show it — rejected: syncing them (duplicate rows unless ids were deterministic and `calendar_links` synced: schema and sync change; follow-up if asked). (3) Per-device switches — rejected: synced switches (calendar tables don't sync). (4) An account that can't be read holds the whole cloud import (R-08; Settings shows Reconnect / "Couldn't reach") — rejected: importing the others (the unread account's events would look removed). (5) Instances of repeating Google/Microsoft events can write back (their instance ids address one occurrence); meetings someone else organises (Microsoft `isOrganizer` false) and read-only calendars can't (`ExternalEvent.editable`). (6) Placeholder client ids/secrets (`placeholder…`) count as unset: Connect then says the server isn't set up. UI: Settings › Calendars gains the account groups (switches, Disconnect with a confirm, Reconnect), GOOGLE AND MICROSOFT with the two Connect rows, the cross-device note, and signed out "Sign in to connect Google or Microsoft"; SEND CHANGES BACK lists device and cloud calendars together; web no longer shows "arrives in a later update" (onboarding O4 on the web points to Settings). `LinkLauncher.open(sameWindow:)` for the web. Golden `settings_page_calendars_google_*` (derived); `settings_page_calendars_*` regenerated (the disabled web button is gone). Smoke-tested against local Supabase: start → Google URL with PKCE, the callback with a bad code → back to the app, the state single use, `calendars` → []. Owner gate: consoles, secrets and function deploy in 08 §6.1.
- [x] M7.5 Travel-time buffer suggestion for events with a location. (R-17) Notes: no routing/maps service (it would send the place off the device: a cloud endpoint and a new dependency), so the suggestion is a rule on the device — `travelSuggestion` (`domain/calendar/travel_time.dart`, pure): Settings › Calendars › TRAVEL TIME › Suggest travel time on (off by default, docs/02 "if the user enables it"), an imported timed event with a place, not started, planned, no travel time yet → `Settings.travelTimeMin` (5 min–2 h, default 15). The place: `ExternalEvent.location` (already read by the plugin, the Mac bridge and both providers) is cleaned at the import boundary (`importedLocation`: control characters out, ≤ 200, blank → null) and kept on the device-only link, new local column `calendar_links.location` (Drift schema 4, CHECK ≤ 200; no Supabase migration: links don't sync); refreshed by each import without touching the occurrence, so an accepted travel time survives moves and re-imports. Where: `TravelSuggestionCard` in the event's sheet, "At {place}. Keep 15 min before it for getting there?" · Add 15 min before / Not now (session-only, as Today's prompts). Proposal rule: nothing is written before Add (test); a one-change inline confirm until `ProposalSheet` (M10.2 should migrate it if a day-level "add travel time to these 3 events" flow is wanted). Add writes `travel_buffer_min` through the undoable write path (snackbar + Undo). Shown: the thread row's meta "15 min travel" (and its screen-reader label), the gap before it shrinks by the buffer (`footprintStart`, as `fitAt`/capacity already did), the sheet's Travel time row, and the editor's new Travel time row (every scheduled task, None–2 h; docs/02 F4's editor field, missing until now). Rejected: a separate "Travel" task (a second block to keep in step with the event), learning a length per place (more concepts; follow-up if asked), a Today prompt card (the sheet is where the event is looked at; follow-up). Golden `task_sheet_travel_*` (derived); regenerated `settings_page_calendars*` (new group) and `task_editor_*`, `today_editor_panel_*` (new row).
- [x] M7.6 Include-calendar-in-shift option in drag chip and settings. (R-04) Notes: the drag gets a second chip, "Calendar events too", beside "Shift later tasks" while that is on, only when the day has an imported event a shift could carry; it starts from Settings › Calendars › Move calendar events too (`includeCalendarInShift`, whose subtitle now says "Shared and repeating events stay put"), and Running late keeps following the setting. `ThreadDrag.calendarByDefault` (null hides the chip) and `onDrop`'s last argument is now a `DropShift` (followers, calendar). Write-back decision (docs/03 §3.2): a shift carries an imported event only when its link isn't read-only (`calendar_links.read_only` = read-only calendar, repeating or all-day event, M7.3), domain `movableBlock(o, includeCalendar, readOnlyIds)` threaded through `shiftFollowers` / `runningLate` / `insert` / `moveWithFollowers`; the ids come from `CalendarLinksDao.readOnlyImportIds` (imports with a read-only or missing link) via `readOnlyCalendarIdsProvider`, and while unknown no calendar event moves. A carried event is an ordinary edit: write-back on → `WriteBackController` sends it (test: only the writable one becomes pending); off → it moves here only, as a single drag does. Rejected: carrying every imported event (a bulk shift would leave read-only meetings and series instances silently disagreeing with the real calendar, R-08); carrying only events whose calendar has write-back on (the setting would do nothing until write-back is set up, though a single drag already moves events locally); fixed events stopping the shift like pins (they never did with the chip off; overlap is allowed, R-04). Goldens regenerated: `shift_chip_*` (new row), `settings_page_calendars*` (subtitle). `ResizeGrip` moved to its own file (thread_drag.dart stayed under 300 lines).
- [x] M7.7 `/ship-check`. Done 2026-10-24. `dart analyze --fatal-infos` and format clean; 2,596 tests (1 skipped: the reference sheet); coverage `lib/domain` 96.8 %, overall 91.0 %; local Supabase restarted, `supabase db reset` + `supabase test db` 53/53, the `supabase`-tagged client tests incl. `delete_account_test` green (the edge runtime comes up with `supabase start`). Device tests (`tool/device_tests.sh`): Android API 37 emulator 12/12 — the device suite (app_launch, onboarding, journey, deep_link, background_refresh, notification_exactness **20/20 fired, 0 duplicates**, sweep) and the new calendar checks; iPhone 17 Pro simulator 6/6 calendar checks. The 08 §6.2 M7 gates are now integration tests (`calendar_prompt`, `calendar_denied`, `calendar_import`, `calendar_write_back`, `calendar_revoked`, iOS `calendar_regranted`; 08 §5b): Connect/explain/prompt (iOS usage text)/denied/allowed (M7.1); import in the calendar's colour within a resume, repeats, all-day, moved there → moved here with the subtask kept, deleted → gone / Trash, switched off → gone, link → browser (M7.2); move/resize/rename sent with notes, alerts (and on Android invitees) kept, repeating changes here only and the editor says so, clash banner Keep mine / Use calendar's, revoked → "1 change hasn't reached your calendar yet" → Try again (Android) / sent on the next launch (iOS, which ends an app whose access changes) (M7.3); a shift with Calendar events too carries and sends the one-off event, never the repeating one (M7.6). Fixed from it: the async calendar providers watched other providers after an `await`, so a permission re-read on resume threw `UnmountedRefException` as an uncaught error (found on Android; regression test `calendar_providers_test`). Security §9 walked: one migration in M7 (0005 calendar OAuth: RLS + pgTAP, tokens in Vault behind service-role functions), gitleaks/osv green in CI, no dependency or CSP change, imported titles/notes/places validated at the import boundary and Edge Function inputs checked against the caller's accounts, logs carry ids/codes only, imports are device-only and Disconnect is confirmed, deep links unchanged, export/deletion unchanged (deletion re-checked on local Supabase). CI on `milestone/m7`: app (checks, goldens, a11y-web, integration-android, the four builds), supabase and security green. Owner gates added to 08 §6.1: the Mac calendar checks (status still `notAsked`; the macOS prompt needs a person) and two real-iPhone checks (Open settings lands on Dottoday's page — the iOS 26 simulator opens Settings' top level; invitees kept on write-back — the simulator can't hold an event with invitees). Open (release milestones, as before): `docs/parity-checklist.md`, the offline / two-device scenarios on devices, the screen-reader passes.

## Milestone 8 — Export, feedback, release hardening

- [x] M8.1 Export JSON/CSV/ICS + Import JSON (backup before import) on all platforms; share sheet / file save. (R-11) Done 2026-10-24. Notes: the formats, validation and backup service existed since M1.8/M2.4; this adds the way in and out. Settings › Export (also File › Export on the Mac): Everything (JSON) / Tasks (CSV) / Calendar (.ics) each build from one consistent snapshot (`data/export/export_service.dart`, files named `dottoday-backup-<date>.json` etc. in the profile's zone, domain `export_files.dart`) and go through a `FileGateway` port (`data/export/file_gateway.dart`, faked in tests): the share sheet on iPhone/Android (which offers Save to Files / Drive), a save panel on the Mac (a folder for the three CSVs, written `.part` then renamed), downloads on the web — `share_plus` and `file_picker`, already in `pubspec.yaml`, no new dependency. The page says export files aren't encrypted (06 §2) before any tap. Import a backup… picks a `.json` (refused over 25 MB before reading), validates it (`decodeExportJson`), then confirms with the file's date, its task count and the replace policy in words ("Anything here that isn't in it moves to Trash. A backup of what's here now is taken first."); nothing is written before Replace (test). Undo restores the pre-import backup. New: ON THIS DEVICE lists the local backups (daily, before an import, before a restore) and a tap goes back to one (confirm + Undo), so the safety backups are reachable. Refusals are named: not a Dottoday backup / from a newer version, too large, or the entity validation message. M7.2 gap closed — imported calendar events and their subtasks are left out of backups and every export, an import drops any an older file holds, and a restore no longer trashes this device's imports (the current state is read the same way), so they keep their links and nothing comes back unlinked beside a fresh import (`backup_calendar_test`; docs/05 "Export format"). Rejected: exporting link keys for re-pairing (links are per device/account; ids would pair with nothing elsewhere), a staging-database swap for restore (restore must go through the write path to sync; one transaction already leaves nothing half-applied), a payload checksum (v1 unchanged; bad files fail parse/validation). Platform exception: the system share sheet / save panel need a person — a manual check is on 08 §6.2 for M8.5. Golden regenerated: `settings_page_export_*`.
- [x] M8.2 Send feedback (mail composer with redacted diagnostics opt-in); in-app What's new; changelog screen. (R-19) Done 2026-10-24. Notes: the sheet (M3d.2) now moves to `features/settings/widgets/feedback_sheet.dart`. Add diagnostics (off by default) shows the exact `diagnostics.json` block before Write email: app version, platform, language, schema version, the install's random sync node id (`hlc_node`), sync state + pending/conflict counts, and counts of tasks/Inbox/done/repeats/Trash/calendar events. `DriftDiagnosticsReader` (data/feedback) is one aggregate query that selects no text column; a canary test seeds a task whose title and notes hold secrets and checks the block holds neither. An unreadable block is named in the sheet and the email goes without it (logged as `feedback.diagnostics_failed`). Still `url_launcher` `mailto:` behind `LinkLauncher`; the app sends nothing. The block goes in the body: `mailto:` can't attach, and the person sees and can edit it there. Rejected: `share_plus` with a `diagnostics.json` file (already a dependency, but the share sheet is not the composer — no recipient or subject on most targets, a download on the web); a mail-composer package (a new dependency, a stop). Recipient: no address exists in the docs, so `.env.example` shipped a marked RFC 2606 `.invalid` address; `Env` reads a `.invalid` address as unset (draft without recipient — rejected: addressing the placeholder, which would bounce) and refuses one that isn't a plain address. Owner gate added to 08 §6.1. Superseded 2026-10-25: the owner chose `feedback@dottoday.app`, now the compiled-in default (`FEEDBACK_EMAIL` overrides); the `.invalid` handling and the gate are gone. What's new: Settings › About › What's new is the changelog page (`/settings/changelog`, a new allow-listed `SettingsPage`; every release's notes, newest first, from `Release` in `domain/release` and one ARB string per release, one item per line — the switch is exhaustive, so a release can't ship without notes). After an update that brought notes, `WhatsNewBanner` shows once above every screen ("Dottoday 0.2.0 is here · See what's new · Not now"); the last seen version lives in `local_meta` (per device, not synced); a fresh install records its version and shows nothing; opening the page counts as seen. Rejected: an automatic sheet after an update (stands between the person and their day; a strip matches the other notices). PRD F11 clarified with these choices. Goldens: `settings_feedback_*` regenerated (new copy), new `settings_feedback_diagnostics_*` and `settings_page_changelog_*`. Platform exception: the composer itself needs a person — manual row on 08 §6.2 for M8.5.
- [x] M8.3 Localisation de/fr/es complete; pseudo-locale test for truncation. Done 2026-10-24. Notes: `app_de.arb`, `app_fr.arb`, `app_es.arb` carry all 954 keys (du / vous / tú; glossary and no-shame words per language in 03 §5.1). `test/policy/l10n_parity_test.dart` is the CI gate for every later task (standing rule at the top of this plan): same keys, placeholders, select cases and line breaks, no untranslated copy outside a per-language list, and `CFBundleLocalizations` (now added to the iOS and macOS Info.plists, without which iOS reports only English) lists every language. `no_shame_copy_test` now scans all four languages. Pseudo-locale: a test-only `PseudoAppLocalizations` (generated from gen-l10n's abstract class by `test/support/pseudo/write_pseudo.dart`, freshness-tested) rather than a pseudo ARB, which would have shipped as a choosable language; harnesses take the locale from `test/support/test_l10n.dart`. `locale_sweep_test` pumps 20 screens (Today, Inbox, Search, Week, Month, Settings + 8 pages, 6 onboarding steps) at 360/700/1280 in pseudo and at the narrowest width in de/fr/es, with Inter loaded: no overflow and no more cut-short text than English (measured afresh — `didExceedMaxLines` reports intrinsic passes too). It found no overflow; the Week board's cards ellipsize by design and are checked for overflow only. `quick_add_hints_test` caught the French Add hint ("1 h" reads as 01:00 in French) — now "1 heure". A new profile starts in the device's first supported language (`deviceLanguageProvider`), else English; existing profiles keep theirs.

- [x] M8.4 Generate `docs/parity-checklist.md` from PRD and verify on all four platforms. (R-13) Done 2026-10-24. Not a fix here: the journey test is phone-shaped (it flings the pager), so the Mac's journey waits for an expanded variant (M8.4b). Owner gates added to 08 §6.1 (Mac notification actions + Mac email sign-in; physical-device feel: haptics, 55 fps on Pixel 6a- and iPhone 12-class phones, real paste, real prompts) and a §6.2 manual line (⌘N / ⌘1–4 / ⌘←/→ in real Chrome). Notes: (1) redo in `UndoService` (`redoable`, `redo`): an undo is itself written as a recorded batch and the rows it overwrote become the one redo entry; a redo comes back as a fresh undo token, so ⌘Z ⇧⌘Z ⌘Z toggles; one level, 30 s from the undo, any new undoable change ends it (rejected a multi-level stack: `latest` is one level too and Trash covers older changes); (2) Edit: Undo · Redo | Delete, no native key equivalents; with a text field focused each acts on the field (`UndoTextIntent`/`RedoTextIntent`; Delete removes a selection only, like native `delete:`), otherwise app undo/redo and `TrashTaskIntent` (moved to `core/shortcuts`) sent to the focus — Today's `TaskKeys` handles it; elsewhere it does nothing; (3) found: the M5.5 root ⌘Z took the key from focused text fields (Flutter's text shortcuts sit above the app's) — `UndoKeys` (⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z, Ctrl elsewhere — the web's parity, no menu there) now turns off while a field has focus (`focusedTextField()`), and so do Today's task keys (⌫, Space and the arrows in a field inside Today); (4) ⌘/ sheet lists ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z under Everywhere. 07 macOS records the behaviour. (2) On a fresh load the first Tab skipped the link, because Today's/Week's autofocused key catcher held focus; the catchers are now `skipTraversal` and `SkipLink` takes the first Tab from a catcher until the link has had focus once (so it never traps Tab); rejected: dropping the catchers' autofocus (arrow keys would need a click first). ## Milestone 9 — See time (goal: the live dot on every OS surface, all showing the same dot within 1 s of a change)

Spec: `11-standout-roadmap.md` A1, B5, B6. Native targets: the existing `ios/DottodayWidgets` extension gains the Live Activity and controls (owner-approved); it needs the App Group, which needs the paid account — build and run on the simulator, device checks go on the owner's gates. Push-to-start Live Activities need APNs from a server and are out (that would send task text off-device — must-ask).

- [x] M9.1 B5 `DaySnapshot`: the M5.4 widget snapshot grows into the one immutable day record — planned + actual (B1), current/next dots, day progress, inbox count, version — written after every write, at Bedtime and on demand; JSON v1 in the App Group (iOS/macOS), `home_widget` storage (Android), `localStorage` mirror for the service worker (web). One format, no second snapshot. **Reference** derived — §B5. **Blocks** B5. **Done when** codec round-trip + hostile-input tests; a test that every write path updates the snapshot; snapshot written ≤ 1 s after a write (perf-tagged). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `WidgetSnapshot` became `DaySnapshot` (`domain/snapshot/day_snapshot.dart`) — not a second format: the same 24 h of dots, now with actual start/end (B1), today's Wake Up/Bedtime (`DayShape.containing`, the weekday's boundary in the profile zone), `current` / `next` (two) and `dayProgress`, plus `currentAt`/`nextAt` so a reader can move on without a rewrite. JSON v1 in `day_snapshot_codec.dart`; the derived fields are written for native readers and ignored on decode; `decodeDaySnapshot` is total (null on another version, mistyped or missing fields, instants outside 2000–2200, more than 20 dots, ids > 64 or titles > 200 chars, durations outside 0–1440). Native readers kept their keys (`v`, `blocks[].start_ms/end_ms/time/until/color/done`, `labels`, `inbox_count`), so the M5.4 widgets and the menu bar read v1 unchanged; `snapshotStoreJson` (was `widgetDisplayJson`) adds the display fields next to the record. Writers: after-write effects (unchanged path), the daily refresh, `BedtimeSnapshot` (one timer to today's Bedtime, re-armed by housekeeping, while the app is open) and `DaySnapshotWriter.write` on demand (M9.2's `WhatsNext`). `test/policy/snapshot_write_paths_test.dart` finds every direct writer of the occurrences table and requires each to schedule the effects (calendar import/write-back), feed `onPulled` (sync) or say why not (Trash purge: rows already out). Perf: p95 ≤ 1 s from commit to snapshot on a 200-dot day (`test/data/perf/snapshot_latency_test.dart`; measured in ms). Web: `WebSnapshotSink` stores it in Cache Storage (`dottoday-day-snapshot`, `__snapshot/day.json`, kept by `sw.js` on activate) with every title blanked. Decisions: Cache Storage instead of the `localStorage` mirror written here — a service worker can't read `localStorage` (rejected: IndexedDB, more interop for one record; posting to the worker, lost when none is running); no titles on the web — browser storage outside the encrypted DB is plaintext (06 §9 "nothing sensitive in localStorage"), and the worker needs ids and times only (rejected: encrypting with the vault key, which the worker can't unwrap without the page). CI's web-DB probe now writes the snapshot and scans Cache Storage and `localStorage` too (0 hits locally). macOS: the menu bar gets the record in process; an App Group on the Mac waits for a reader outside the process (M9.5 decides the Mac widget). docs/04 §4 rewritten for B5.
- [x] M9.2 B6 intents and the action queue: exactly five — `AddTask`, `WhatsNext`, `MarkDone(occurrence)`, `AddFifteen(occurrence)`, `StartFocus(occurrence)`. Apple: App Intents in Swift (app + extension); Android: static shortcuts + widget/notification broadcast actions; Mac: the menu bar calls the same Dart use-cases; web: service-worker actions. When the app isn't running an intent updates the snapshot optimistically and appends to a shared **action queue**; the app drains the queue through the write path on launch, resume and the background job (same queue the watch will use, ADR-0010). **Reference** derived — §B6. **Blocks** B6. **Done when** queue tests (idempotent replay, an action for a deleted task is dropped and logged by id, order kept); each intent has a Dart use-case test; Swift intents unit-tested in `RunnerTests`. Split 2026-10-25 (one platform per commit):
  - [x] M9.2a Dart core. Done 2026-10-25. `domain/intents/`: `DotAction` (sealed: `AddTask`, `MarkDone`, `AddFifteen`, `StartFocus`; `WhatsNext` is a read and never queued), `QueuedAction` + JSON (`kind` `add_task`/`mark_done`/`add_fifteen`/`start_focus`, `id`, `at_ms`, `occurrence_id`, `planned_end_ms`, `text`; ids must be UUIDs, lowercased — Swift writes capitals — so they are safe to log), `DotIntents` (the five use-cases, through the single write path; ids trusted only for the current profile) and the `ActionQueueStore` port. Idempotent by construction, no applied-id ledger: `AddTask` uses the action id as the task (or series) id and stops when it exists; `AddFifteen` carries the end the surface showed and applies only while the task still ends there (`droppedStale` otherwise, `alreadyApplied` at +15); Done and Start are no-ops when already so. `ActionQueueDrainer` applies in queue order, stops at a failed write (later actions never overtake it), drops a deleted/unknown task's action and logs `intent.dropped` with ids and the reason only, removes unreadable entries, and re-reads before removing so entries appended mid-drain survive. Drains on launch and resume (housekeeping, isolated so a bad queue can't block the refresh) and in the background job. Stores: `home_widget` key `action_queue` on iOS (App Group) and Android, Cache Storage `__snapshot/actions.json` on the web, none on the Mac (the menu bar runs in process and calls `DotIntents.run`, M9.6). Rejected: an applied-id ledger in `local_meta` (a second thing to keep in step; the actions can carry their own guard); dropping actions that fail to write (the user tapped Done — retry instead).
  - [x] M9.2b Apple: App Intents in Swift for the app and the widget extension (`AddTask`, `WhatsNext`, `MarkDone`, `AddFifteen`, `StartFocus`), each appending to the App Group queue and updating the snapshot optimistically; unit tests in `RunnerTests`. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `ios/Shared/` is compiled into Runner and DottodayWidgets (`tool/ios_add_shared_intents.rb`, idempotent): `DotStore` (App Group keys), `DotQueue` (append; lowercase UUID v7 ids, `UUIDv7`), `SnapshotPatch` (Done ticks and sets `actual_end_ms`, Start sets the first `actual_start_ms`, +15 moves `end_ms`/`duration_min` and rewrites "until …" from the snapshot's new `format` block — zone, language, 12/24 h, the localised pattern — so the widget shows the tap at once), `DotActions.perform` (queue + patch + `WidgetCenter` reload + a Darwin notification) and the five `AppIntent`s (`MarkDone`/`AddFifteen`/`StartFocus` take the occurrence id and are hidden from Shortcuts until M9.5 adds phrases; `WhatsNext` answers from the snapshot's own localised labels; `StartFocus` opens the app). `IntentQueueBridge` in AppDelegate turns the Darwin notification into `queueChanged` on `dottoday/intents`, so a running app drains at once. Intent strings in `ios/Shared/<lang>.lproj/Localizable.strings` (en/de/fr/es; `os_strings_test` checks parity and bundling). `RunnerTests/DotIntentsTests.swift` (7 tests, iPhone 17 Pro simulator) and a new CI step runs them after the iOS build. Rejected: entity-typed parameters (`AppEntity` for dots) — worth it only with Siri phrases, M9.5; a Swift copy of the Quick Add parser (AddTask queues the text; the app parses it with the one parser).
  - [x] M9.2c Android: static shortcuts (Add task, What's next) and a `DotActionReceiver` broadcast for widget/notification Done / +15 / Start that queues and updates the snapshot optimistically. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `kotlin/…/intents/`: `DotQueue` (entries with lowercase UUID v7 ids; the snapshot patch on plain maps — the same rules as `SnapshotPatch.swift`, +15 re-renders "until" from the snapshot's `format` with `java.time`), `JsonMaps` (org.json edges), `DotActionReceiver` (not exported; `pendingIntent(...)` for M9.4/M9.5 buttons; queue + patch in one prefs commit, Glance `updateAll`, then `IntentQueueBridge` tells a running app on `dottoday/intents`). Static shortcuts `res/xml/shortcuts.xml`: Add task → new allow-listed `dottoday://add` (router shows Today and opens Quick Add after the frame; also for M9.5's controls), What's next → `dottoday://today`; labels in en/de/fr/es, and the widget picker names now have de/fr/es too (`values-<lang>/widget_strings.xml`, a gap left from M8.3). `test/policy/intent_wire_contract_test.dart` keeps Dart, Swift and Kotlin on the same queue kinds and keys. Emulator (API 37): both shortcuts registered, the app opens on the link. Decision: no Kotlin unit tests — JUnit would be a new (test) dependency (a stop, CLAUDE.md §10) and the M9.2 done-when asks only for Swift unit tests; the Kotlin rules mirror the unit-tested Swift ones, the wire is policy-tested, and M9.5's killed-app widget test exercises the receiver end to end. Rejected: Android App Actions `CREATE_THING` capability (needs Play-side review and a text slot the static shortcut can't carry; Siri-parity phrases are M9.5).
  - [x] M9.2d Web: the service worker queues Done / +15 from a notification when no tab is open (Cache Storage), and the page drains it. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `web/action_queue.js` (loaded by `sw.js` with `importScripts`, CSP unchanged: same origin) builds `mark_done` from a reminder's Done and `add_fifteen` from `plus15` (the action M9.7 adds) with the end the stored snapshot shows, lowercase UUID v7 ids, and appends to `__snapshot/actions.json` in the snapshot's cache; with a tab open the worker still posts to it as before. The page drains through `WebActionQueueStore` on launch and resume. Tests: `tool/action_queue_test.mjs` (node, in CI next to the push-crypto vector), `test/web/action_queue_store_web_test.dart` (Chrome), the wire contract now covers the worker; PWA check passes (worker controls the page, no CSP violations, offline). Found while checking: the web store's generic `store`/`readStored` refactor hadn't applied — `flutter analyze` sees only the stub behind the conditional import, so only a web build catches it; the web build is now part of this task's check. Decision: Done needs no tab (queue, no `openWindow`); Open and Snooze still open the app — rejected: queueing Snooze (a re-reminder must be scheduled by the app, which isn't running).
- [x] M9.3 iOS Live Activity + Dynamic Island: conic ring, title, "38 min left · 1 of 3", Done / +15 (B6) — exactly `B-ios-live-activity.png`. Started from the app when a dot goes live or is next within 60 min (foreground, resume, notification action), ended on Done or planned end + 30 min; the content state carries the current and next two dots so `Text(timerInterval:)` counts down without updates and the background job refreshes it. **Reference** `B-ios-live-activity.png`. **Platforms** iPhone only here; Android = M9.4; Mac = M9.6; web = M9.7. **Done when** SwiftUI previews/snapshot tests of compact, minimal, expanded and lock-screen layouts in light/dark; runs on the simulator; Done/+15 from the activity reach the database via the queue. Owner gate: Live Activity on a real iPhone. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: Dart decides, Swift lays out. `domain/live_activity/live_dot_plan.dart` is the one lifecycle (also M9.4's): focus = a dot in its planned time, else one overrunning within 30 min of its planned end (not done), else the next starting within 60 min; the next two after it; `nextPlanChange` = the next lead/start/grace moment. `LiveActivitySink` (iOS, with the widgets in a `CompositeSnapshotSink`) runs it on every snapshot and re-plans on a timer while the app is open, sending a fully localised content state (`live_activity_content.dart`: title, colour, icon key, start/end, "9:00 – 11:00", the countdown's words before/after — "38:00 left", "noch 38:00" — "1 of 3" + its label, Done / +15 min, next two) on `dottoday/live_activity`; `sync` with null ends it. Swift (`ios/Shared`, compiled into both targets): `LiveDotState` (+15 re-renders the range from the state's `format`), `LiveActivities` (one activity: request / update / end; stale at end + 30), `LiveMarkDoneIntent` / `LiveAddFifteenIntent` (`LiveActivityIntent`, iOS 17: end or extend the activity at once — +15 only while the end is still the tapped one — then queue via `DotActions`), the layouts in `LiveActivityViews.swift` and `DotLiveActivity` (extension: lock screen + island expanded/compact/minimal). `DaySnapshot` gained `dayDone`/`dayTotal` (today's dots, past ones included) for "1 of 3"; the background refresh engine registers the bridge too. Tests: Dart lifecycle (7), content in en/de + sink start/re-plan/end (4); RunnerTests render the four layouts in light and dark with `ImageRenderer` and compare with `RunnerTests/LiveActivitySnapshots/*.png` (≤ 0.5 % pixels off; missing = recorded), plus the state's +15; `integration_test/live_activity_test.dart` on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator: a live task starts the activity, the activity's +15 path twice adds 15 once, its Done path ends it at once and the queue ticks the task in the database. Decisions: "1 of 3" = today's dots done of all (the render's pie; no-shame: a count, no colour for what's left) — rejected: the dot's position in the day (the pie would mean nothing); task icons as the nearest SF Symbols (`TaskSymbols.swift`) — rejected: a Swift SVG-path parser for the app's glyphs; in tests the countdown and thread are drawn frozen (an offscreen render can't draw a live timer or `ProgressView(timerInterval:)`), on a device the system draws them live, so the knob on the thread shows only in frozen renders. Owner gate unchanged: Live Activity on a real iPhone (08 §6.1 E). Found by the device test and fixed (an M9.2 bug): the drain removed settled entries with two `home_widget` calls (read, then write), so an action queued in between — the Live Activity's Done does exactly that — was overwritten and lost; `NativeActionQueueStore` now asks the native side (`dottoday/intents` `readQueue` / `removeQueued`) to remove in one locked step (Swift `NSLock` around append/remove; Kotlin `synchronized` with the receiver), falling back to `home_widget` only in an engine without the channel (Android's background refresh). Swift test for the atomic remove; Dart test for +15, +15 again, then Done. The count hides when today counts no dots (no "0 of 0").
- [x] M9.4 Android ongoing notification: the live dot as an ongoing notification on the reminder channel with a count-down chronometer, title, "1 of 3", Done / +15; same lifecycle as M9.3; posted by `NotificationSync` (a live-dot slot), never ad hoc. **Reference** derived — §A1 ("Android ongoing notification … mirror of the Live Activity card in M3 style"). **Done when** planner tests for the lifecycle; emulator screenshot in the sweep; actions work with the app killed (queue). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: the same plan and content as M9.3 (`liveDotPlan`, `liveActivityContent`); `LiveActivitySink` now serves both platforms (iOS `dottoday/live_activity`, Android `dottoday/ongoing`). Kotlin `OngoingDot`: one notification (id 3335) on the task-alerts channel, silent after the first post (`setOnlyAlertOnce` + `setSilent`), count-down chronometer to the planned end (to the start when coming up, "starts in" beside it), title, "9:00 – 11:00 · 1 of 3", Done and +15 min as `DotActionReceiver` broadcasts, tap → the task, `setTimeoutAfter` to planned end + 30 so it leaves by itself. The receiver removes it on Done and re-posts it with the end moved on +15 (range re-rendered from the content's `format`, `DotQueue.plusFifteenContent`). Device checks on the API 37 emulator: `integration_test/ongoing_dot_test.dart` (posted → +15 → 45 min in the database → Done → done, notification gone) and `tool/ongoing_killed_check.sh` (app killed with `am kill` + SIGKILL, notification still there, Done tapped in the shade — no Flutter engine runs — notification gone, then the app opens, drains and the task is done). uiautomator never sees the shade idle while the chronometer ticks, so the script takes `DONE_TAP` (the Done button's position, read off a shade screenshot once per device). Decision: posted by the snapshot's live-dot sink, not inside `NotificationSync` as written here — `NotificationSync` plans scheduled reminders and never sees the snapshot, while the surface rule (CLAUDE.md §11) says surfaces read `DaySnapshot`; one sink owns it, so it is still never ad hoc. Rejected: posting through flutter_local_notifications (its action handler needs a Dart isolate; the receiver works without one). Emulator screenshot of the shade joins the sweep in M9.9.
- [x] M9.5 Interactive widgets + controls: Next up with a Done button (iOS 17 AppIntent button, Android `home_widget` interactive callback → queue), Today thread (medium/large, read-only), Inbox count — the M5.4 widgets upgraded, not duplicated; iOS 18 Control Center / Action button controls "Add task" and "Start focus"; Android Quick Settings tile (Kotlin `TileService`) with the same two; Siri / App Shortcuts phrases for the five intents. **Reference** derived — §A1 (interactive widgets, controls), widget geometry from the M5.4 goldens. **Platforms** iPhone + Android; Mac: WidgetKit Next up gets the same Done button (macOS 14+); web: n/a (covered by M9.7). **Done when** widget timeline/snapshot tests; Done from a widget ticks the task with the app killed (simulator + emulator). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: Next up gained a ✓ (iOS 17 `Button(intent: MarkDoneIntent)`; Android a Glance `actionSendBroadcast` to `DotActionReceiver` — the receiver is this repo's "interactive callback": it queues and patches the snapshot with or without the app; rejected: home_widget's background Dart callback, which needs a Dart isolate per tap); its label comes from the snapshot (`labels.done`). Today and Inbox unchanged (read-only). iOS 18 controls in the extension (`DotControls.swift`): Add task (`OpenQuickAddIntent` → `dottoday://add`) and Start focus (`StartFocusIntent` with no task = the dot under way, else the next); both available for Control Center, the Lock Screen and the Action button. Android Quick Settings tiles (`tiles/DotTiles.kt`): Add task and Start focus (starts the current or next dot through the receiver, opens it; unavailable when nothing is on). Siri / App Shortcuts: `Runner/DotShortcuts.swift` with a phrase for each of the five intents, translated in `Runner/<lang>.lproj/AppShortcuts.strings` (policy: every phrase in every language, each with the app-name slot); MarkDone / AddFifteen / StartFocus now take an optional task and fall back to the dot under way (`DotActions.currentBlock`). `Snapshot.swift` moved to `ios/Shared` so RunnerTests cover the widget timeline (`WidgetSnapshotTests`: live/next/boundaries, the current-dot rule). Killed-app proof: `tool/widget_killed_check.sh` (simulator: task written, app terminated, the MarkDone entry the widget writes put into the App Group, app opened — task done) and `tool/ongoing_killed_check.sh` (Android, the same receiver the widget calls); a script can't tap a home-screen widget, so finger-on-widget checks are on 08 §6.1 E. `intent_wire_contract_test` pins the widgets, controls, tiles and phrases to the B6 paths. Decision: Mac — no WidgetKit Next up here: there is no Mac widget extension (M5.4 chose the menu bar, PRD F9), and adding one is a native target (a must-ask, CLAUDE.md §11); the menu bar gets Done / +15 in M9.6. Raised for the owner at the milestone close.
- [x] M9.6 Mac menu-bar dot: the M5.5 "Next up" item becomes the day dot that fills from Wake Up to Bedtime plus next up; click → What's next menu with Done / +15 (B6). **Reference** derived — §A1 ("Mac menu-bar dot"). **Platforms** Mac; web has no menu bar (M9.7). **Done when** golden of the rendered dot image (0/50/100 %, light/dark menu bar); menu actions tested. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `macos/Runner/DayDotStatusItem.swift` (was `NextUpStatusItem` in AppDelegate; added by `tool/macos_add_day_dot.rb`): the status item's image is `DayDotImage` — a template ring filled clockwise from the top by the share of the waking day (`wake_ms` → `bed_ms` from the snapshot), a dot inside — so the menu bar tints it for light and dark itself; the title is still Next up ("Deep work · until 11:00"). It redraws at every start/end and at least every 5 min for the ring. The menu is What's next: the dot (opens it), Done and +15 min (labels from the snapshot, `labels.done` / `labels.plus`), what is left of the day, Today. Done / +15 go back on `dottoday/status_item` as `action` and the app runs them in process (`mac_menu_actions.dart` → `DotIntents.run`; the Mac has no queue — the menu bar lives with the app). Goldens: macOS RunnerTests render the template tinted on a light and a dark bar at 0 / 50 / 100 % (`RunnerTests/DayDotSnapshots`, ≤ 1 % pixels off; the sandboxed test host can't write the source tree, so a missing reference is recorded in its temp folder), plus the day-share rule; new CI step runs them on the macOS build. Dart: the menu's arguments → intents, and Done / +15 through the write path. Decision: the image is drawn in Swift as a template (rejected: a Flutter-rendered PNG sent over the channel — a coloured image can't follow the bar's tint, and a timer would round-trip every 5 min).
- [x] M9.7 Web live dot (parity, R-13): the tab title "● 38 min left · <title>" and a favicon ring while a dot is live, the Badging API for Inbox count where supported, and the dot-start web notification with Done / +15 via the service worker. **Reference** derived — §A1 web. **Done when** widget tests for the title/favicon generator; Chrome check in the sweep. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `data/live_activity/web_live_dot.dart` (pure): while a dot is live (`liveDotPlan`), the title "● 38 min left · Deep work" (the Live Activity's words, `durationMinutes` for the time; overrunning, just "● Deep work"), a favicon ring — 32 px SVG as a `data:` URL (CSP `img-src data:` already allows it), the elapsed share drawn from the top in the dot's colour — and the Inbox count for the badge. `webLiveDotStateProvider` recomputes it every minute from the last snapshot (`LastDaySnapshot`, set by the web sink in memory with titles; the stored copy has none); `MaterialApp.onGenerateTitle` shows the title (so Flutter's own title updates don't fight it), `applyTabLiveDot` (js_interop) sets the favicon and calls `navigator.setAppBadge` / `clearAppBadge` where the Badging API exists. Reminders on the web now carry Done and +15 (`NotificationAction.plus15`, web only — iOS/Android/macOS keep Done/Snooze/Open; Chrome shows two actions, a tap opens the task); `NotificationResponder` gives +15 with the tab open, `web/action_queue.js` queues it with none (M9.2d); the server-push payload carries the same two. Tests: the generator (en/de, overrun, nothing live, badge, ring share), +15 in the responder and the push payload, a Chrome test that sets and restores the favicon; web release build. Decision: the title goes through `onGenerateTitle` rather than writing `document.title` (rejected: Flutter rewrites the title on rebuild).
- [x] M9.8 Consistency gate: a test harness changes a task (Done, +15, rename) and asserts the snapshot, Live Activity content state, Android ongoing notification, widgets and menu-bar model all show the same dot ≤ 1 s later; surface-contract policy test (M6.5.11c) covers the new Swift/Kotlin sources. **Done when** green in CI (Dart side) and on both simulators (native side). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: Dart (CI): `test/data/surfaces/surface_consistency_test.dart` runs the real write path with the real surface sinks (the stored snapshot JSON with the native readers' live rule replayed on it — what Swift's widgets, Kotlin's widgets and the Mac menu bar do — the Live Activity / ongoing content state through `LiveActivitySink`, and the web tab via `webLiveDot`) and asserts after a rename, +15 and Done that every surface shows the same dot (id, title, end), or none after Done, within 1 s. Device: `integration_test/surface_consistency_test.dart` reads back the widgets' stored snapshot (App Group / home_widget prefs) and the live surface (new read-backs: iOS `activeStates`, Android `activeContent`) after write, rename, +15 and Done, polling to 1 s: iPhone 17 Pro simulator 325 / 170 / 162 / 116 ms; Android API 37 emulator 337 / 519 / 305 / 228 ms. Found and fixed: (1) +15 compared ends to the microsecond — a task made in the app can carry microseconds the database drops, so +15 was refused as stale; now compared in whole milliseconds (what every surface carries), with a test; (2) Android's rename took 911 ms because the composite sink wrote the widgets (three Glance redraws) before the ongoing notification — the surfaces are independent, so `CompositeSnapshotSink` now writes them at once (rename 519 ms), still isolating failures. Surface contract (M6.5.11c) now covers `ios/Shared`, the Kotlin `intents` and `tiles`, `lib/data/live_activity`, `lib/data/intents` and the Mac's `DayDotStatusItem.swift`, and fails on a missing folder instead of skipping it. The device suite's default list gains `surface_consistency` and the platform's live-dot test.

Device suites (`tool/device_tests.sh`, default list now with `surface_consistency` and the live-dot test): Android API 37 emulator 9/9 and iPhone 17 Pro simulator 9/9 — app_launch, onboarding, journey, deep_link, background_refresh, notification_exactness (20/20/0 on both), surface_consistency, ongoing_dot / live_activity, sweep; killed-app: `tool/ongoing_killed_check.sh` (Android) and `tool/widget_killed_check.sh` (iOS) pass. Native-surface sweep: Android shade with the ongoing notification (`build/sweep/m9/`), Chrome `tool/a11y/web_live_dot_check.mjs` (new, in CI a11y-web: title "● 39 min left · Live check", favicon ring; Today at 1280 / 390, light / dark); Live Activity / Dynamic Island / lock screen by RunnerTests renders (simulator screenshots don't show the island); Mac menu bar by its goldens and tests — the Mac run stopped at the first-start Keychain prompt of the renamed app, which only the owner answers (08 §6.1 E). Four-platform comparison: `docs/parity-checklist.md` F9.1 web and F9.2 ✓ on all four. CI `app` green on `feb63c6` (all four builds, iOS and macOS RunnerTests, goldens, integration-android, a11y-web), `supabase` and `security` green. 06 §9: no table (RLS n/a, pgTAP green); gitleaks green; every new input validated (queue entries: UUID ids, known kinds, text ≤ 10,000, total decoders; snapshot decoder; menu-bar arguments; native readers read only app-written JSON); no task content in logs (drainer logs `LogId`s and outcomes) or payloads (notification payloads unchanged; the web's stored snapshot has no titles — CI probe scans Cache Storage and localStorage); nothing sensitive in localStorage (none written); destructive actions: none added (Done and +15 are undone in the app); no dependency added (pubspec, Gradle, npm unchanged); deep links: `dottoday://add` allow-listed, intents act only on the current profile's ids; CSP unchanged (favicon `data:` is already allowed, `importScripts` same origin, no inline script); account deletion and export untouched (`delete_account` supabase test green). 08 §6: parity ✓ above; accessibility — every new native control labelled (island/lock-screen buttons, widget ✓, tiles, menu items) and the sweep's a11y-tree checks pass on both simulators; offline — every surface reads local data only (surface contract); sync conflict — unchanged, supabase tests green; notifications — exactness 20/20/0 on both.

## Milestone 10 — Start (goal: help with the thinking before a task reaches the thread; nothing applies before Accept)

Spec: `11-standout-roadmap.md` B1–B4, A3; ADR-0009.

- [x] M10.1 B4 `ThinkingService` (`breakDown`, `planFromText`, `suggestDuration`) returning structured proposals, never writes. Backends chosen at runtime: **on-device** — Apple Foundation Models (iOS/macOS 26+, Swift guided generation behind a platform channel), Gemini Nano via ML Kit GenAI / AICore (Android, supported devices; the Gradle SDK is covered by the owner's on-device approval — recorded in the commit), Chrome's built-in Prompt API where present (web) — then **rules** (saved breakdowns, history medians, a 3-step template). **Cloud** and **bring-your-own-key** backends exist only as interfaces behind a feature flag that is off, with no provider wired; choosing one is a stop (must-ask). Prompts/outputs stored locally only; Settings › Privacy line explaining the paths. **Reference** derived — §B4 (on-device chip as `B-ios-break-it-down.png`). **Blocks** B4. **Done when** contract tests run every backend through one fake; availability detection tested per platform; a policy test that no backend file makes a network call; < 2 s on-device on the simulator/emulator where the model is available (perf-tagged, skipped with a reason where it isn't). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: domain `lib/domain/thinking/` — `ThinkingService` holds no repository (policy test: the domain thinking code imports no write path) and returns `Result<proposal, ValidationFailure>`; order: **breakDown** saved breakdown (no model) → on-device → empty 3-step template sharing the length in 5-min blocks; **planFromText** on-device → Quick Add's `parseMany` (R-07); **suggestDuration** history median when ≥ 3 samples (real length from `actualStart`/`actualEnd` when both exist, else planned) → on-device → thin history. Depth: a request carries the level being split (0 = task), allowed 0–2 so steps land at ≤ 3. `OnDeviceThinkingBackend` is one Dart class over a `ThinkingTransport` for all four platforms: one prompt set (`thinking_prompts.dart`, JSON Schema per kind, the user's text quoted as data, no-shame tone), a 10 s stop for a stuck model, `ModelOutput` validating the answer as untrusted input (JSON only, control characters out, titles ≤ 200, minutes clamped to the column checks, lists capped), and every exchange journaled. Transports: `dottoday/thinking` → `ios/Runner/ThinkingBridge.swift` (Foundation Models, guided generation with `@Generable` types mirroring the schemas; the same file compiled into the macOS Runner, `tool/xcode_add_thinking.rb`) and `thinking/ThinkingBridge.kt` (ML Kit GenAI Prompt API `com.google.mlkit:genai-prompt:1.0.0-beta4`, owner-approved on-device library, pinned while beta; FeatureStatus → available/downloadable/downloading/unavailable); web `PromptApiTransport` (`LanguageModel.availability/create/prompt` with `responseConstraint`). Cloud and own-key: `RemoteThinkingBackend` interface only, `ThinkingFlags.remoteBackends = false`, the service asserts none is passed, a policy test fails on any implementation. Local only: saved breakdowns (100 titles) and the prompt log (last 50) in `local_meta` (encrypted, never synced or exported); Settings › Privacy › Help with thinking says the paths, which one this device uses now, and Clear thinking history. Model proposals are 2–5 steps (`defaultMaxSteps`; the sheet holds up to 7): 7 steps took ~1.9 s on the simulator, too close to the 2 s target. Tests: contract suite runs iOS/macOS/Android/web/rules through one `FakeThinkingTransport` (valid proposals, never throws, garbage/timeouts → fallback, journal, wire prompt); service order/validation/depth; `ModelOutput`; `LocalThinkingMemory`; channel availability per platform's words; Chrome test of the Prompt API transport (stub + the browser's own API); `test/policy/thinking_on_device_test.dart` (no network in any thinking source, Dart/Swift/Kotlin; no write path; no remote implementation). Device (`integration_test/thinking_test.dart`, perf-tagged, in the device suite): iPhone 17 Pro simulator available, breakdown 1.55–1.70 s; Mac available, 1.33 s; Android API 37 emulator unavailable (no AICore) → timing skipped with the reason. Decisions: history before the model for lengths once it has 3 samples (the user's own time is the promise) — rejected: model first (generic guesses over real data); `local_meta` for the saved list and log — rejected: a new Drift table (schema bump for two bounded JSON values). Note for the owner: ML Kit pulls Google's `transport-*` libraries, which ML Kit uses for its own usage metrics; prompts and answers stay on the device.
- [x] M10.2 B3 `ProposalSheet(before, after, edits)`: current thread at 35 % alpha, the proposed thread over it with outlined accent dots, an editable change list, Accept / Discard, "Nothing is saved until you accept"; Accept = one write-path transaction + 30 s Undo; Discard writes nothing. A reusable `expectNothingWrittenBeforeAccept` test helper (counts write-path batches and `change_log` rows). **Reference** `B-ios-break-it-down.png`, `B-ios-rebalance.png` (shared chrome). **Blocks** B3. **Platforms** iPhone/Android bottom sheet; Mac/web the right-hand `TaskEditor` panel (§4: no sheets at expanded). **Done when** goldens (empty, 1, 7 edits), the helper used by M10.4, M11.1, M11.4, M12.5. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: model `domain/proposals/proposal.dart` — `Proposal(userId, before: DaySnapshot, edits)` in the one snapshot format (B5); `after` is derived from `before` + the remaining edits, so × on a line redraws the proposed thread without it (rejected: passing `after` separately — two sources that can disagree); each `ProposalEdit` carries its change (shifted / resized / movedToDay / added / untouched / steps), the dot before and after, and the rows Accept writes; `toBatch()` merges them (a row named twice is written once). UI: `shared/proposals/` `ProposalSheet` (header with origin chip and ×, subtitle, body card, option chips, Accept, Discard, "Nothing is saved until you accept", the save-failure banner with Try again) and `ProposalThreadList` (legend now/proposed, ghost dot at `TaskAlpha.ghost` 35 % under the proposed dot outlined in the accent, times with the old ones struck, title, a chip per change kind whose text says it, × with "Keep … as it is", Bedtime). Opening: `core/editing/active_proposal.dart` (`ActiveProposal`, like `EditingTask`) so any feature opens one through a provider; `features/proposals/ProposalHost` sits once in the app shell: full-height sheet on phones (popover from the medium width), the right-hand panel at the expanded width (`DotSize.sidePanel`, Esc discards, the task editor panel steps aside until it closes). Accept: `data/proposals/ProposalAcceptor` → `UndoService.applyUndoable` (one write-path transaction) + the 30 s Undo snackbar; a second tap while writing does nothing; a failure keeps the sheet. A flow's own editable body plugs in through `ProposalRequest.body` (Break it down, M10.4). Tests: `test/support/proposal_harness.dart` — `expectNothingWrittenBeforeAccept(tester, harness, open:, edit:, leave:)` over a real in-memory database and write path, counting write-path batches (`CountingWrites`) and `change_log` rows on open, while editing and after Discard/×/Esc; used here for the sheet, × and the panel. Flow tests: Accept writes the remaining lines once, Undo puts them back, failure + Try again, nothing left → Accept off, a11y guidelines. Goldens `proposal_sheet_{empty,1,7}` × light/dark × compact/medium/expanded × 1x/2x (the real host: sheet, popover, panel). New tokens: `TaskAlpha.ghost`, `DotSize.sidePanel` (the editor panel now uses it).
- [x] M10.3 B2 step durations in the editor: each subtask row gets an optional duration chip and keeps its order (drag + Move up/down); "Length = sum of steps" switch (`durationFromSteps`), with a typed override. **Reference** derived — §B2 + the M3f subtask editor. **Done when** domain tests for the sum/override; editor goldens. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: domain `scheduling/step_lengths.dart` — `stepsTotal` (live steps with a length; null when none has one), `followSteps` (length = the sum, clamped to 5 min – 24 h, while `durationFromSteps` is on; unchanged when no step has a length), `setFollowSteps` (the switch), `overrideLength` (a picked length wins and turns following off — the "typed override"). Editor: `StepLengthChip` (`shared/duration/`) on each subtask row in the editing list — "15 min" or "+ time", pill on iPhone and 8 px corners elsewhere (§4), a tap opens a choice sheet (No time, 2 min … 2 h); the row keeps drag + Move up/down. Under DURATION, "Length = sum of steps" (adaptive switch) with "Steps add up to 45 min", disabled with "Give a step a time to use this" until a step has a length; shown only when the task has steps. When a step's length changes the editor's draft follows (didUpdateWidget, written by the normal autosave). The editor's debounce/flush moved into `DraftAutosave` (`editor_writer.dart`) and the title into `EditorTitleField` to keep `task_editor.dart` at 300 lines. Scope: occurrence-level (`occurrences.duration_from_steps`); the series flag and template step lengths follow when Break it down writes steps to a series (M10.4). Tests: domain (sum, follow/clamp, off keeps the length, override), `editor_step_lengths_test` (chip writes and clears a step length; switch waits, follows, re-follows; a picked length overrides), editor tests now scroll to the footer. Goldens: new `task_editor_steps_*` (timed steps, switch on), regenerated `task_editor_*`, `today_editor_panel_*` (chips + switch row). Decision: a picked duration turns following off — rejected: keeping the switch on and ignoring the pick (a control that silently does nothing).
- [x] M10.4 B1 Break it down: a button in Add step 1 and the task sheet → `ThinkingService.breakDown` → `ProposalSheet` with the steps as a mini thread, per-step durations, depth ≤ 3, "Add a step", total "the dot grows to fit"; "My usual breakdown" saved per title and offered first (no model); degrades to the saved list or a 3-step template. **Reference** `B-ios-break-it-down.png`. **Platforms** sheet on phones, panel on Mac/web; Android chips at 8 px radius (§4). **Done when** goldens of the mini thread at 1, 3, 7 steps; nothing-before-Accept test; depth cap tested; accepted steps tick off in Focus with timers. Done 2026-10-25. Notes: flow `features/task_editor/breakdown/` — `breakDown(context, BreakdownTarget)` asks `ThinkingService.breakDown` (the usual breakdown first, no model; then on-device; else the empty 3-step template) and opens the `ProposalSheet` with: origin chip ("on-device" / "your usual", none for the template), the task's row above the card (`BreakdownLead`: outlined dot, title, "Today · 4:00 PM · was 1 h 30 → now 1 h 40"), the mini thread as the body (`BreakdownBody`/`StepTile`: numbered rings in the task's colour with a segment as long as the step, editable titles with "Step n" hints, `StepLengthChip` per step — read-only sum on a step with sub-steps — chevron to open a step: sub-steps, "depth 2 of 3", Break this down, Remove step; drag or Move up/down; "Add a step" up to 7) and under it "Total 1 h 40 · the dot grows to fit" with the before → after rings and the Smallest step note. Depth ≤ 3: `BreakdownTree` (`domain/thinking/breakdown_tree.dart`) refuses a split past level 3 and the service refuses depth ≥ 3 requests; a split asks the model one level down (a template is no help there). Accept for a saved task (`domain/proposals/breakdown_proposal.dart`): the leaves become its subtasks after the existing ones, the task follows their sum (`durationFromSteps`), one write + Undo; Accept for Add step 1 (`draftOnly`, `overSheet` so it shows above Add's popover on wide screens): the steps join the draft (`AddDraft.steps`, length grows to the sum) and are written with the task on Save — its subtasks, or a repeating task's step list (`subtaskTemplate` with lengths, `durationFromSteps`). Every Accept saves the leaves as the title's usual breakdown (local_meta). Entry points: Add step 1 under the title; the task sheet's and the Mac/web panel's Subtasks header via a new root `BreakDownIntent` (features don't import each other); not the phone editor, whose open draft would miss the new length. Android chips 8 px via `dotChipShape`. Decisions: B2's step model is flat (11 §0, no parent column), so depth lives in the proposal and Accept writes the leaves, a sub-step titled "Enter income · Salary" — rejected: a `parent_id` column (a second schema change beyond the owner's 0006 approval, and Focus/Smallest step want one ordered list); model proposals stay ≤ 5 steps (M10.1) while the usual breakdown and added steps go to 7. Found and fixed in `ProposalRequest.copyWith`: an update dropped `lead`, `below` and the flags (guard test `active_proposal_test`). Tests: tree (sum, depth cap, leaves/join/cut, edits, cap 7), breakdown proposal (order, growth, Inbox task), Add draft steps (one-off, picked length wins, series list), flow (`expectNothingWrittenBeforeAccept` for the task sheet path and the Add path; Accept writes, Undo snackbar, usual breakdown offered next time without the model; split to depth 3 and no further; template waits for titles; wide Add → popover; a11y), task sheet → `BreakDownIntent`. Goldens: `breakdown_mini_thread_{1,3,7}` × light/dark × compact/medium/expanded × 1x/2x; regenerated `add_*`, `task_sheet_*`. "Accepted steps tick off in Focus with timers" is M10.5's acceptance (Focus is built there).

Opening sets `actualStart` (M6.5.2). The Live Activity continues on leaving. **Reference** `B-ios-focus.png`. **Platforms** full screen on phones; on Mac/web a centred Focus view in the content area with the rail kept, Esc to leave. **Done when** goldens both themes three widths; reduced motion stops the ring animation; DND never silences the alarm channel (test). Done 2026-10-25. Notes: `features/focus/` — `FocusView` (top bar ‹ Today · FOCUS · ⋯ Task details; `FocusRing` 160 px in `shared/dot/`, the planned time gone clockwise in the task's colour around its icon, eased per second and still under reduced motion; "9:00 – 11:00 · 2 h", title, "38 min left"; `FocusStepCard` "STEP 2 OF 3 · Draft timeline · 14:20 left" with its bar; `FocusStepThread`; the two toggles side by side, stacked from 1.3× text; Done / +15 min / Next step; "Dot starts still break through Focus (time-sensitive)"). State is pure (`domain/focus/focus_state.dart`): the first step runs from the real start (B1) or the planned one, a later step from the tick that finished the step before it (even if Focus opened later), never negative (0:00, no "late"). Opening runs the `StartFocus` intent (B6 → `markStarted`, once). Done (with Undo, ends Deep work), +15 (with Undo), Next step (ticks the current step) go through the undoable write path; failures say so with Try again. Where: phones and narrow windows push `/focus/:id` full screen (allow-listed deep link); at the expanded width `FocusFrame` shows it in the content area over the tab (an always-present `IndexedStack`, so the tab is never remounted), rail kept, Esc leaves — opened through core `FocusTask`/`openFocus`, as `EditingTask`. Entry points: one tap on the live dot (`LiveRow.onFocus`; its done circle and Done still tick), the `StartFocus` intent from controls/tiles/Siri: the queue drainer asks the root to open Focus for a StartFocus queued in the last 2 min (`FocusRequest`), so one path serves iPhone (the intent opens the app) and Android (the tile now opens Today and the drain opens Focus). The Live Activity is untouched, so it continues on leaving. Chime: "every 15 min" plays the bundled chime through the existing sound player while Focus is open; off by default, remembered per device (`local_meta`). Deep work: Android `focus/DeepWork.kt` on `dottoday/deep_work` sets Do Not Disturb to alarms-only (only that level is accepted; never total silence, never touching a DND the user set, only undoing its own) until the dot's planned end (`DeepWorkOff` alarm) or Done, after notification-policy access (new `ACCESS_NOTIFICATION_POLICY` permission; Settings opened, status re-read on resume); iPhone/Mac: apps can't switch Focus, so the row opens a sheet explaining the Shortcuts way (Start focus + Set Focus, from the Action button/Control Center/Siri) with Open Shortcuts; web: no switch, "Use your computer's Do Not Disturb". `taskView` moved from the timeline feature to `data/tasks/` (re-exported) so Focus can watch a task. Tests: domain focus state (6) + countdown; `focus_view_test` (the render's numbers, start once, Next step, +15, Done leaves and ends deep work, chime twice then off, Android until/access, Shortcuts sheet, web row, reduced motion → zero-length ring tween); `focus_frame_test` (content area, tab kept, Esc; phones unaffected); thread: live dot opens Focus, other dots tick; drainer: fresh StartFocus opens Focus, stale/missing don't; deep links; `policy/deep_work_alarms_test` (alarms-only level, Kotlin never NONE/PRIORITY or a policy change, dot-start alarms are alarm-usage audio — so DND never silences the alarm channel). Goldens `focus_*` light/dark × compact/medium/expanded × 1x/2x. Accepted Break it down steps tick off here with their timers (M10.4's last acceptance). Decisions: Deep work's Do Not Disturb stays on after leaving Focus until the dot ends (the roadmap's "off when it ends") — rejected: off on leaving (switching screens would end it); iOS StartFocus opens Focus through the drained queue — rejected: opening the URL from the intent (the shared Swift file also builds into the widget extension, where `UIApplication` is unavailable). Owner gate (08 §6.1 E): Do Not Disturb on a real Android phone; the Shortcuts automation on a real iPhone.

Built as the shared trigger the deferred escalation will reuse. **Reference** derived — §B2 ("Smallest step reminder/row"). **Done when** planner tests (fires once, cancelled by Start/Done/move); row golden. Smallest step adds: steps to do and none ticked yet (`stepsNotStarted` — a ticked step means it is under way), the first open step offered (`smallestStep`). Reminder: the planner adds one `NotificationPurpose.smallestStep` per such occurrence at the trigger (slot 9, stable id, quiet hours → silent); `NotificationSync` passes which occurrences qualify (new optional `SubtaskRepository`), and the OS gateway looks up the step at post time, so the text is current and a rename re-posts (content hash). Shown as "Just Gather documents · 10 min?" ("Just X?" without a length) over the task's title on the alerts channel, one Start action everywhere: iOS/macOS category `smallest_step`, Android action, the web's in-tab and server-push notifications, and the service worker queues it as `start_focus` with no tab. Start opens Focus (`/focus/:id`), which records the real start (B1) — from the reminder (responder) and from the row. Cancelled by a real start, Done or a move (a move re-plans it at the new start + 10); never more than once (re-planning is a no-op). Never a modal, never on the first move.
